# Forge 1.12.2 port investigation

Investigated on **2026-11-11**. Target: Minecraft **1.12.2**, **Forge**, and **Java 8**.

## Recommendation

Use **plain `1.20.1` as the starting point for the easiest modern backport**. It has the modern mod's core gameplay, direct NBT persistence, older rendering interfaces, and substantially less code to translate than `1.21.5`. This is still a substantial platform port: changing the Minecraft version or replacing Fabric's Gradle plugin cannot make it work on 1.12.2.

The local branch **`codex/1.12.2-forge`** starts at `1.20.1` commit `319a4ea0160d6600cffcd5f5cc9e363ceaab44a1`. The initial investigation is commit `e51116f9`. The progress section tracks changes to the Forge port since that commit.

Use **`1.20.1-lts` as the main reference for newer features and fixes**, and `1.21.5` for changes after LTS. If full recent feature parity becomes the overriding requirement, reconsider LTS as the implementation base before doing substantial work. LTS retains Java 17 and the older renderer, but incorporates much of the newer gameplay and an emulation of the component/codec architecture. Starting from plain 1.20.1 prioritizes time to a working port; it does not imply that newer features are permanently excluded.

There is also an old Forge implementation on `master`. Reuse its ideas where helpful, particularly rendering and Forge integration, but do not assume it is a working 1.12 precursor. Its declared build and source APIs disagree. A classic-feature revival from that branch might be competitive, but establishing that would require a separate build/runtime experiment.

## Port progress

The parity baseline is the pinned [plain 1.20.1 snapshot][plain]. The Forge branch builds `src/main/java` and `src/main/resources`. Use `git show 319a4ea0:<path>` to inspect the donor's original files. Keep each in-progress feature open until its behavior is verified.

### Cycle 1 · Forge build and first registered item

- Built the stock Forge MDK and then the port with Zulu 8u472. Pinned Forge 14.23.5.2864, ForgeGradle 3.0.197 and Gradle 5.6.4. The port build ran `reobfJar` successfully and packaged Forge metadata, the MIT license and the first item. Updated the GitHub workflow to use Java 8 and build the jar. CI has yet to run remotely.
- Launched the packaged jar using the official Forge installer in an isolated runtime under `run/production`. The client log recorded five mods loaded, creation of a new world and a player joining. The registry dump listed `psychedelicraft:coffee_beans`. The user's screenshot showed the Psychedelicraft tab and the bean sprite in game. The tested jar's SHA-256 was `32f92d076fa928bcc7f1488393073523bc3758f924f1f092d4bb4105209489b3`. The final build contained identical class and resource bytes, with Java 8 class version 52.
- The ARM Java client launch failed with `UnsatisfiedLinkError` because `liblwjgl.dylib` lacked ARM code. Temurin x86-64 8u504 under Rosetta reached OpenGL and ran the packaged client. The two Java 8 runtimes are local tools outside the repo.
- The initial ForgeGradle client run under x86-64 failed at `NetworkRegistry.newChannel:207`. `javap` showed an extra `BUKKIT` enum constant in the generated binary and only `CLIENT` and `SERVER` in the installer jar. Resolving the mapped sources and rebuilding produced a `-recomp.jar` with the two expected constants. `resolveForgeSources` provides the source-resolution step. The server retry used the recompiled jar. [Matching ForgeGradle issue](https://github.com/quat1024/modern-forge-1.12-template/issues/2).
- The dedicated development server reached the Minecraft EULA prompt. Its local `run/server/eula.txt` still requires the user's acceptance before server tests can proceed. The separate production server is installed for later jar tests.

### Cycle 2 · Mushroom ingestion, persistent effects and state packets

- Added both mushroom items with the donor food values and independent influences. Translated the 1.20.1 effect and influence math to Java 8. Preserved the five-tick influence cadence, 15-step ingestion delay, per-tick decay, red and brown modifier formulas, and wake-up reset.
- Attached a Forge capability to players. It writes desired and active strength plus the remaining influence and delay to NBT. Non-death clones copy this state. The server sends snapshots on consumption updates, login, respawn, dimension changes and tracking start. The client applies them on its game thread to the matching player in the matching world.
- `./gradlew --no-daemon clean build` passed under Java 8, including reobfuscation and six JUnit tests. The tests cover onset timing, compressed NBT and continuation after load, the two modifier profiles, wake-up, expiration and malformed saved influences. The production jar has Java 8 bytecode and excludes the test classes.
- The packaged jar with SHA-256 `ef94a19db5b603125c3af232f519008674f3a21257be6296f2f7558da9860cd6` passed four runtime checks on the installed Forge 14.23.5.2864 client. A separate test mod created a disposable survival world and invoked the normal right-click and finish-use callbacks at full hunger. One item was consumed, the server effect started after 75 ticks, the saved player file held active and pending NBT, and the local client received the effect. Attempts to automate the held mouse click timed out. This run verifies the item callbacks, integrated-server state and owner sync. The local log is `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle2/callback-pass.log`.

### Cycle 3 · Color post-processing and camera wobble

- Ported the donor's full weighted hallucination pool and its selection, buildup and fade rules. Four color effects now consume the selected strengths. The remaining slots preserve the donor's selection distribution while their renderers are being ported.
- Ported the donor's slow and quick channel rotations, intensification and desaturation to GLSL 1.20. The client applies them before the HUD with a separate color target and restores the main framebuffer and render state. When F1 hides the HUD, the last world pass supplies the hook. The shader reloads with resources and the target follows the main framebuffer size.
- `CameraSetup` applies the donor's rotating nonuniform scale to the world camera. Both camera and color effects use the local player's synchronized state. The renderer clears its per-player hallucination state when the world or player changes.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed. All eight JUnit tests passed, including inactive and stacked hallucination selection followed by decay to zero.
- Tested the packaged jar (SHA-256 `fa2e8257d66a827a844051334c11c1f96b1c69fa91e6a93c6427cb87f9d8c9c5`) on installed Forge 14.23.5.2864 with Temurin x86-64 8u504 and the Apple M4 Max OpenGL 2.1 driver. A separate test mod sampled a known framebuffer pixel and the live modelview matrix in a disposable integrated world. Stacked server-side mushroom doses changed the sample from `(180, 44, 92)` to `(171, 50, 92)` and changed the camera matrix by a maximum component difference of `0.038228787`. Resetting the server effects restored both baselines.
- The same runtime test observed the shader rebuild after resource reload, the color target resize from `854×480` to `950×544` through Minecraft's resize handler, and continued processing at `1920×1080` after a fullscreen toggle. The hidden-HUD world pass also changed the sampled pixel. The first fixture timed out waiting for its OS window change to reach Minecraft's resize handler. The retry exercised that handler directly and toggled fullscreen through the game. The local pass log is `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle3/runtime-render.log`. Other graphics drivers, dedicated multiplayer and the remaining visual passes still need coverage.

### Cycle 4 · Tall crops and drying tables

- Ported planting, growth rates, bonemeal, height limits and age-dependent harvests for cannabis, hops, coca, tobacco and coffea. The tall crop metadata stores age and, for tobacco and coffea, the upper-section flag. Natural cannabis has its own block ID and shares the cultivated model, drops and crop-height calculation. Its separate ID preserves the wild soil rule alongside all 16 age values. Wild crop placement, potted plants and exact explosion-decay drop probabilities remain open.
- Added wooden and iron drying tables, crafting recipes using Forge's `plankWood` OreDictionary entry, the donor screen and on-table item rendering, and server-side drying. A full set of nine matching inputs produces three items in the output slot. The tile saves inventory, heat and progress, exposes heat to comparators and progress to weak redstone, and supports side inputs and top output through Forge item handlers and the sided inventory.
- Ported both mushroom batches and the cannabis leaf, cannabis bud, tobacco and coca batches. Drying keeps the donor's 19,200-tick wooden and 14,400-tick iron defaults at maximum heat. Custom recipe loading and the three batches for peyote, belladonna and jimsonweed remain open. Coffea cherries smelt to coffee beans in the vanilla furnace, which uses 1.12's 200-tick cook time. The donor recipe uses 100 ticks.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed. Thirteen tests passed after adding two automation regressions. They cover unique four-bit metadata and state round trips for each crop, coffea's two age limits, drying heat, the vanilla hopper's slot limit and Forge handler simulation and remainders.
- The packaged jar (SHA-256 `65c4e39be83bc254ea54ed1537fcb4e60c067ffab727093a39c26a5c0dc32bf5`) passed 15 checks in a disposable survival world on installed Forge 14.23.5.2864. The fixture planted, fertilized and harvested all five crops, ran the wild cannabis random-tick callback on dirt and resolved every registered crop state and item to a baked model. It also matched the coffea smelting recipe and crafted and placed both drying tables.
- The fixture shortened drying times for runtime testing. The real client screen rendered the nine slots and accepted shift-click packets. The wood batch processed nine brown mushrooms and transferred three results. The test also observed comparator heat, progress power, exposed rain reset, and a tile NBT round trip with nine items and partial progress. The iron table rejected a mixed batch, accepted side input through the vanilla hopper path, refused top insertion, produced three red magic mushrooms, supported simulated and partial extraction, and dropped its remaining items on break. All four crop-ingredient batches completed. The first runs exposed a shift-click remainder moving slots and the 1.12 hopper merging into occupied inputs. Both regressions passed after correction.
- Local evidence is in `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle4/runtime-crops-drying.log` and `run/production/client/screenshots/cycle4-table-screen.png`. Tile progress after a full world restart and dedicated multiplayer remain unverified.

### Cycle 5 · Succulents, nightshades and plant foods

- Ported jimsonweed, belladonna and tomato planting, growth and harvesting. Shears take leaves from immature stages and fruit from ripe stages. Using bonemeal on a ripe plant harvests its fruit and restores the ripe stage through the vanilla item use. All three fruit-to-seed recipes produce six seeds.
- Ported agave and peyote stage, substrate and selection-box behavior. Agave shearing takes leaves and steps back one age. Moving through agave at age one or older, interacting empty-handed, and breaking without an appropriate tool use cactus damage. Nightshades and agave use horizontal and vertical motion damping for the 1.12 collision hook. Exact movement over multiple ticks and explosion drop parity remain open. Peyote drops one item plus its age and has four textured tile render stages using the donor cuboids.
- Added drying batches for peyote, belladonna and jimsonweed. Tomatoes restore food and can be eaten at full hunger. Dried peyote and belladonna berries use the existing server influence pipeline. The donor registers atropine using its LSD effect class. The port implements its direct color intensification, thresholded color and movement contributions, hunger modifier, stroke damage and caffeine mitigation. Caffeine's remaining sensory and attribute behavior, the drinks that apply it, and atropine's bloom, sound, movement attributes and tremble consumers remain open.
- `clean build` passed on Java 8, including reobfuscation and 18 JUnit tests. New coverage includes age metadata, mature growth refusal, peyote yield by age, direct intensification and caffeine interaction.
- The survival fixture used Forge's normal block-use path to plant and fertilize the five crops, shear all three nightshades, harvest ripe fruit with bonemeal, and shear agave. It verified agave's cactus damage on movement, empty-hand use and breaking, plus crouch protection and one collision's per-axis damping. The first two attempts at empty-hand verification hit the player's initial spawn immunity and the interaction manager's empty-stack `PASS` result. The final check ran after spawn protection and measured health directly.
- The fixture also preserved the peyote tile through growth, checked that bonemeal remained unchanged at maturity, counted four items from a mature break, and completed the three drying batches at a reduced test duration. The client rendered all four peyote stages. Early client logs showed unresolved agave particle aliases. The port now references the existing fan sprite directly in each stage, and the fixture checks that each plant has a baked model and a resolved particle sprite.
- The edible-item checks used the normal right-click and finish-use callbacks at full hunger. Both plant influences became active on the server after onset and reached the owner client. Belladonna alone changed a known framebuffer sample from `(180, 44, 92)` to `(186, 40, 105)` while the randomized color modifier was zero. A loaded overdose state triggered stroke damage, and the same test state with caffeine lowered atropine strength before it dealt damage. Caffeine acquisition, dedicated multiplayer and ordinary crop generation remain unverified.
- The final packaged jar passed 13 runtime checks on Forge 14.23.5.2864. Its SHA-256 is `4a60f47b0f32deabcf97a967de359382c1419c9429c1247986790e9b160f7bc3`. Local evidence is in `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle5/runtime-plants.log` and `run/production/client/screenshots/cycle5-peyote.png`.

### Cycle 6 · Lattices, morning glory and grapes

- Added lattice crafting, fuel and flammability, the donor's horizontal connection rules and vertical placement orientation, and separate collision and ray-trace boxes for the center and arms. Four connection bits are saved as block metadata. Grown lattice tiles save age and the decorative flag, and send changes to the client to rebuild the multipart model. Waterlogging remains open. The 1.12 selection outline uses the enclosing box, while hit detection and collisions use the individual arms.
- Ported morning glory stem growth, spread onto lattices, and fruit and seed yields. Connected vines advance and spread, and lose age when separated from a rooted stem. A bounded search checks stem connectivity through neighboring crop lattices. Grapes convert a plain lattice without consuming the held item, matching the donor. Grapevines grow without a stem. Each crop uses the donor's shearing yield and regrowth stage. Pick block and Silk Touch preserve the item's age, and item placement creates a decorative lattice. Grape food uses the donor's 15-tick use time and one-point food value.
- Ported the donor lattice and stem models, all four held stages for each vine, sprites, both translations, and the two crafting recipes. Morning glory's suspicious stew interaction needs the 1.12 equivalent of the newer vanilla item.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed all 22 JUnit tests. The four new tests cover metadata, rotation and mirroring, lattice ray traces and stem age. Natural crop generation, potted plants, and the remaining player-effect consumers are still open.
- The packaged jar passed ten checks across two fresh Forge 14.23.5.2864 client processes. The survival fixture crafted the lattice from mixed planks and six seeds from one flower, checked 700 ticks of fuel for bare lattice and zero for the decorated items, and placed a corner and a vertical copy. Both vines established, ripened, spread and yielded three or four items when sheared. The fixture also exercised grape food, age-preserving pick and Silk Touch, decorative harvest refusal, and morning glory decay when the stem was removed.
- The client selected the expected textures for both crops and all eight item variants, then switched a planted morning glory lattice to the ripe texture after a live tile update. A fresh client reopened the saved world and verified the growing and decorative tile ages, the persistent flag and both connection masks. An earlier in-process test also restored state but logged stale vanilla packets after fixture-driven unload. Both final processes exited normally.
- The tested jar's SHA-256 is `e096b7f2626b25208e793c5ab882f43fb8010a611ab02ab50919f70a3a7bf258`. Local evidence is in `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle6/runtime-lattices.log`, `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle6/runtime-lattice-reopen.log` and `run/production/client/screenshots/cycle6-lattice.png`.

### Cycle 7 · Wild crop generation

- Added eleven plant patches to Forge's Overworld biome decoration hook. Tall patches use a 1-in-160 placement chance, a circular patch with radius one to three, mature lower sections and randomized tops. Coca requires water within one block of the origin's soil. Single plants use the donor's 1-in-20 chance and five offset attempts. Placement uses a seed derived from the world, chunk and feature and leaves Forge's decoration random stream available for vanilla and other mods.
- All five tall species use wild block IDs with the existing age, model and harvest rules. The wild IDs preserve the donor's soil rule alongside the four bits of metadata. Newly grown upper sections use the matching cultivated crop. The donor's rooted substrate update uses coarse dirt in 1.12.
- Mapped the donor's forest, hill, plains, cold, roofed forest, jungle and dry categories to 1.12 biomes. Flower Forest and Sunflower Plains support morning glory. Meadow and Lush Caves have no native 1.12 biomes. Enable flags and inclusion/exclusion lists use legacy biome IDs, the supported vanilla tags and Forge BiomeDictionary types. Agave shares peyote's setting as in the donor.
- `clean build` passed on Java 8 with reobfuscation and 26 JUnit tests. New coverage includes wild crop age and soil rules, legacy biome defaults, inclusion conjunctions and exclusion precedence.

### Cycle 8 · Juniper and potted plants

- Ported the forked trunk and layered canopy for juniper sapling growth and Overworld decoration. Players can harvest berries and keep the supported leaf. Shears and Silk Touch preserve the leaf type. Leaf waterlogging and exact explosion decay remain open.

Cycle 110 adds a dedicated stripping cue. Both wood recipes are loaded through vanilla crafting, and juniper planks join the Forge plank ore dictionary.
- Added all seven donor potted plant models and localized block names. Right-clicking a vanilla empty pot with a supported plant inserts it. A second interaction returns it and restores the empty pot. Existing vanilla potted flowers continue to use their tile entity. Modded filled pots store their plant in the block ID.
- A clean Java 8 build with reobfuscation passed all 30 JUnit tests. Four new tests cover both leaf states, pillar axes and rotation, sapling stages and juniper's biome defaults.
- The packaged jar passed eight checks on the installed Forge 14.23.5.2864 client. In a fresh default-terrain survival world, native decoration generated a juniper in the included Plains biome. The fixture ripened and harvested berries, exercised shearing and leaf decay, stripped both wood forms, and checked the recipes, fuel values and berry food settings.
- All seven pots filled and emptied in survival. In this build, removing support dropped both the pot and the plant. Cycle 107 updates that behavior to match the donor. The client controller sent insertion and removal through the integrated-server connection, and the client received the block and inventory updates. The client resolved all seven models and rendered a grown tree and filled pot before saving and exiting. The first fixture run asserted a server interaction return value that 1.12 reports as `PASS` for an empty hand even after block activation. ### Cycle 10 · Standing and wall juniper signs

- Added the 16-angle standing sign and the four wall orientations, the donor's sign texture and item sprite, and a four-line editor for each side. Breaking a support drops the corresponding sign. A juniper-plank recipe yields three signs, and mixed planks still craft vanilla signs.

Pouring reads the source before the target in crafting slot order and returns the partially filled or empty source as a remainder. The village profiles now produce the authored coffee mugs, peyote cups and syringes with their original quantities.
- Coffee, peyote juice and caffeine injection use the donor serving sizes and influence parameters. Coffee warmth affects the caffeine dose and queues the warmth influence when present. - Ported the flask and distillery with 8,000 mB tanks, vessel slots, native GUI and the donor models and textures. The distillery updates its outlet when adjacent receivers change and connects to flasks, barrels, vat edges and distilleries that do not point back. Its GUI reports a missing or blocked receiver.
- Fermented, unaged brews can gain up to sixteen distillation levels. A normal step transfers 1 mB of slurry and preserves the brew's remaining volume and attributes. Flower extracts follow the donor's 2:1 ratio through two stages and retain their batch data. The receiver gets canonical slurry, following the LTS behavior. A missing, full or incompatible receiver pauses the timer and leaves the tanks intact. Fire and lava use the donor heat rates. Magma uses the LTS rate.
- The native recipes accept `ingotCopper` from the Ore Dictionary. The port supplies copper ore in new non-flat Overworld chunks because stock 1.12 has no copper. The ore generates in natural stone around Y48, drops raw copper, and smelts in a vanilla furnace. Ore generation can be disabled in the Forge config. Crafting a distillery from a filled flask preserves its fluid. Machine block items can also carry fluid through Forge capabilities and the pouring recipe.
- A clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed 61 JUnit tests. New tests cover brew distillation volume and stage limits, flower-extract ratios and tint, machine-item capability simulation and NBT, and all six distillery facings. The first runtime check exposed a Forge tile reset on outlet changes. Preserving the tile on same-block state changes fixed it, and the suite passed 62 tests with the added regression check.
- The packaged Forge client found copper in a new default-terrain Overworld, harvested raw copper and smelted an ingot in a vanilla furnace. Both native recipes worked. Upgrading and placing a filled flask preserved its tagged 6,000 mB batch. The distillery connected automatically, waited for an incompatible receiver to be emptied, then processed faster over lava and deposited 1 mB of slurry. Harvesting and replacing the flask preserved that slurry, and removing the receiver paused the timer. A separate linked pair processed two flower-extract stages. Inspected the linked models, liquid surface, steam and distillery progress in the client screenshots.
- A fresh Forge process restored the outlet, both tanks and the active timer, then completed the second step with 5,998 mB of tagged brew and 2 mB of slurry. A vanilla hopper supplied the flask with an empty bottle, and the survival client shift-clicked the partially filled bottle out once. The tested jar's SHA-256 is `44449fb3e6aa10d5c92be03cb8601d827253587ee45040591f11b5b295d50116`. Local evidence is in `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle19/runtime-distillery.log`, `runtime-distillery-reopen.log` in the same directory, and `run/production/client/screenshots/cycle19-distillery-world.png` and `cycle19-distillery-gui.png`.

### Cycle 20 · Furnace coffee and fluid serving effects

- A Forge loading plugin extends the native furnace recipe lookup for coffee vessels. Each 200-tick smelt raises the coffee one heat level, keeps the vessel and volume, and preserves the vessel name and fluid attributes. The lookup uses the same warmth attribute as the renderer and consumption code. Warm and hot outputs use Forge's item experience hook.
- Ported the drink and injection rules for brews, coffees, teas, kava, peyote juice, concentrates and flower extracts. All fifteen brew profiles retain their fermentation, distillation and maturation strengths. Vinegar follows LTS and grants no alcohol influence. Ketchup grants sugar. Existing fluid NBT and serving sizes carry through the same item use path.
- Added active gameplay behavior for alcohol, kava, cannabis, cocaine, bath salts, LSD, sugar and sleep deprivation. Implemented movement, attack and mining speed modifiers, bed refusal, wake effects and the donor's disabled-by-default `doSleepDeprivation` rule. The existing renderer receives the supported camera and color inputs. Hand tremble, heartbeat, double vision, motion blur and stomach integration still need their own hooks.
- The Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed 67 JUnit tests. The five new tests cover heat progression and NBT in two vessel types, eligible serving routes, stage-scaled effects, vinegar and effect restoration.
- On packaged Forge 14.23.5.2864, the native glass recipe still resolved and a vanilla furnace smelted copper. The furnace heated a tagged cup twice. The client shift-clicked the output and drank it in survival. Warmth activated first and caffeine followed. The survival client drank part of a fermented brew, used a syringe and drank cannabis tea. The server applied the movement modifier and the client received caffeine, alcohol, cannabis and bath salts. The first serving fixture timed out because it expected warmth and caffeine to overlap. The passing fixture checked each effect when it was active.
- A fresh Forge process restored the tagged brew remainder, empty tea cup and the three active effects from the brew, tea and injection. The client received all three effects on join. Both final processes saved and exited normally.
- The tested jar's SHA-256 is `000c677bb17534841478ce2acfee18485edf343c5a716661fac09f80567fcc17`. Local evidence is in `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle20/runtime-core-smoke.log`, `runtime-servings.log` and `runtime-servings-reopen.log` in the same directory, and `run/production/client/screenshots/cycle20-hot-coffee.png`.

### Cycle 21 · Placed drinks and bottle rack

- Wooden mugs, stone cups, shot glasses, chalices and bottles can be placed on surfaces. Each block has a 16×16 placement grid with stacks up to five vessels high. Empty-hand pickup finds the closest occupied cell within two grid steps and returns the top vessel. Each vessel retains its rotation, name, dye and fluid data. Survival placement consumes one vessel. Creative placement keeps the held copy.
- Ported the four-facing bottle rack and its stick-and-plank recipe. Each of its nine visible slots holds one bottle. The horizontal faces expose the rack to Forge item automation. Its second donor bottle type, the Molotov, depends on that item's later port.
- Both displays use the donor's ground models, fill heights and colors. The resource manager loads the donor geometry settings at model bake and on reload. Forge sends the stored stacks to tracking clients through tile update packets. The 1.12 placement sound uses glass and the rack uses wood.
- The Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed 70 tests. Three new tests cover the five-vessel cap, exact top retrieval and NBT copy isolation, bounded grid lookup, the four rack facings, and one-bottle simulated and real transfers.
- The packaged Forge survival client placed a tagged coffee cup and inserted a tagged bottle in the rack. The fixture stacked all five vessel types, refused a sixth in the same cell, and picked the top vessel. The native recipe accepted mixed planks, and a side-facing vanilla hopper stocked another rack slot. The client resolved all ten vessel meshes and all four rack models, rendered both displays, and resolved them again after a resource reload. The first fixture timed out because it placed the hopper on the drink display's block. Moving the hopper to the rack's opposite side corrected the test setup.
- A fresh Forge process restored both displays with the same facing, stack order, vessel names, fluid volumes and custom attributes. The survival client retrieved the targeted mug and bottle. A moving pig knocked down the five remaining vessels exactly once, and breaking the rack dropped its remaining bottle. Both runs saved and exited normally.
- The tested jar's SHA-256 is `4b3fe3573df22c8c8d7c7d0d59974507debc41043fa88999e71fd3d9fb0399c7`. ### Cycle 22 · Physical fluids and buckets

- All 27 mod fluids use Forge fluid blocks with eight flow levels and an optional tile entity for attributes. Horizontal spread follows the donor's viscosity, and falling liquid propagates downward. Flow inherits attributes from the feeding block. The port uses LTS's finite-source rule to conserve tagged batches.
- Custom buckets pour one full 1,000 mB source. An empty vanilla bucket recovers the source with its brew and custom data. Dispensers use the same source rules. Partial buckets stay usable in machine transfers. Custom liquids evaporate when poured in dimensions where water does. Coffee sources cool and retain other attributes.
- Forge's fluid mesh uses the still texture, flowing texture and color for the actual batch. The client receives the attributes through tile updates. Water-tagged donor liquids use Forge's water contact hooks and form obsidian, cobblestone or stone on lava contact. Slurry uses tinted 1.12 bubble sprites and a lava-pop sound. Visual QA for the bubbles and fog is pending. Waterlogging, drip particles and remaining water-tag integrations are open.
- A clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed 72 tests. Two new tests cover the flow-level and tile metadata bits, source collision checks, and the shared bucket and tile attribute format.
- The packaged Forge client refused to pour a 375 mB bucket, poured a full tagged brew and rendered the batch's appearance. A vanilla bucket recovered exactly 1,000 mB and left the second empty bucket in the stack. Flow receded after the source was collected. A redstone dispenser poured and collected the same tagged fluid. The first fixture failed pickup while the player stood in the flowing liquid. The retry aimed from a raised block and verified the target on both sides of the connection.
- Scheduled ticks spread honey one block at level five and carried coffee three blocks downward. Invoking coffee's random tick handler twice cooled it and preserved its batch tag. Isolated fixtures formed all three lava-contact products. A Nether pour returned an empty bucket and left the target empty. A fresh Forge process restored the tagged source and flow, including the batch and brew stages.
- The tested jar's SHA-256 is `dc6e829eaf42d42cc3510b942b6a5e2292757c0ca193d62cad2c7fcd552fd0e9`. Local evidence is in `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle22/runtime-fluid-final.log`, `runtime-fluid-reopen.log` in the same directory, and `run/production/client/screenshots/cycle22-fluid-reopen.png`.

### Cycle 23 · Appetite, overeating and paper bags

- Player food stats use the donor's appetite and suppression rules. Vanilla hunger, saturation, exhaustion and regeneration retain their native values. The projected hunger and saturation drive the HUD and eating eligibility. Health packets carry the raw values so the client applies the projection once. The capability saves the appetite lock and the overeating score accumulated by servings that exceed the real food cap.
- Reaching the donor's overeating threshold causes nausea. An empty off-hand paper bag captures the episode. Unbagged vomiting uses the donor's sound and temporary drops with infinite pickup delay. The LTS countdown fix guarantees that the episode ends. Added the operator command `/vomit [player]` and the donor's damage source for repeated unbagged episodes.
- Added the paper bag recipe, storage, slot clicks, ground pickup, adjacent-slot collection, manual emptying and dispenser use. A bag stores a single item variant with its tags and an integer count. The allowlist maps available vanilla and mod items into 1.12 and will gain the remaining donor items as they are ported. Paper bag sounds use 1.12's leather sound. While the client is tripping, a bag of vomit can display one of the donor's five vanilla food forms.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed 76 tests. Four new tests cover the hunger projection and raw packet boundary, saved overeating, large tagged bag counts, bottle capacity and emptying.
- The packaged jar passed the previous world-fluid fixture with the new player and container hooks loaded. In survival, a real caffeine injection suppressed eating at raw hunger two while the client displayed three. With appetite increased, the client ate a steak at raw hunger twenty. The meal crossed the overeating threshold, caused nausea and filled the off-hand paper bag.
- The native inventory screen transferred named cookies both ways between a bag and the cursor or slots. The client collected dropped cookies with a stack of empty bags, and the selected bag absorbed matching cookies from the neighboring slot. Right-clicking emptied those eight cookies. A redstone dispenser ejected one named cookie from a separate bag. The first fixture clicked the ground before the dropped item reached the client. Waiting for the item on the client verified the normal interaction.
- An operator ran `/vomit` without a bag and the episode expelled non-pickup items before ending. The client resolved a disguised bag to a vanilla food model and name. A fresh Forge process restored the appetite lock, raw hunger, partial overeating score, named cookies and bag of vomit.
- Repeated unbagged episodes caused the localized overeating death on the seventeenth. Native respawn reset the stomach state and restored the owner's hunger meter.
- The tested jar's SHA-256 is `1076c189e54f4ae81ef299c17ad421213c9c5c8c1dfafdedf97b8223f0cd5ec2`. ### Cycle 25 · Smoking tools and Harmonium

- Added the smoking pipe and bong, their shaped recipes, and Harmonium crafting and dyeing. A 30-tick draw takes one item from the first eligible inventory slot. The pipe uses inhaled doses and the bong uses immediate doses. The server rechecks the slot at completion. The pipe lasts 50 draws and burns for 200 ticks in a furnace. The bong lasts 128 draws. The pipe fits in a paper bag. The pinned placement tag admits the five drink vessels and excludes the smoking items.
- Dyed Harmonium stores its tint in `display.color`. The Forge balancing setting defaults to off and controls whether all sixteen dye variants appear in the creative inventory. Dealer offers that use this setting are pending. Both the pending influence and active color survive NBT round trips and use the existing player sync.
- Added empty, loaded and active bong models and the pipe's active model. The 1.12 player model applies the donor's raised arm angles to the active hand for smoking items and powders. The bong uses the legacy lava-pop sound during a draw because 1.12 has no bubble column sound event.
- Harmonium's effect applies the donor's depth-based contrast and two-direction colored bloom. Forge's 1.12 framebuffer stores depth in a renderbuffer, so the renderer copies the scene depth into a sampled texture before vanilla clears it for the hand pass. It releases the texture when the world or resources change. Randomly selected bloom and contrast, exact smoke motion and audio playback verification are open.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed 81 tests. Two new tests verify delayed colored influence after a save and the persistence of mixed active dye colors.
- The packaged Forge client verified all three crafting recipes, dye crafting with a named input, the creative setting, and the pipe's fuel value. In survival, the pipe refused to draw from an empty inventory, then used exactly one ingredient from the earliest eligible slot. Removing the only ingredient mid-draw left durability and effects unchanged. The off-hand bong used one dyed Harmonium and transmitted its immediate effect with the exact color. Both active models and arm poses resolved and rendered.
- The initial production launches exposed a missing parameter in the release client's stack frames and an immutable ASM frame-local list. The final transformer preserves the argument in an added local with updated frames. A framebuffer check also caught that the hand pass had cleared the world depth. The final run read distinct terrain and sky depths, including `0.9889462` for the wall at screen center, and rendered dye contrast and bloom without the earlier sampler warning. The fixture saved and exited normally.
- The tested jar's SHA-256 is `c6fee013eb99fcb15441a5f676b4e9a2f647bde8ff0756f596ba45d30696018e`. Local evidence is in `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle25/runtime-smoking-world-depth.log` and the four `cycle25-*.png` captures under `run/production/client/screenshots`.

### Cycle 26 · Villager careers and acquisition

- Registered Forge dealer, addict and unemployed professions. Ordinary spawn selection preserves the native pool, with the nitwit share divided between nitwits and unemployed villagers. Greenhouse residents start unemployed. Adults can claim an unoccupied wooden or iron drying table to become dealers. The villager and table save the assignment, and a dealer who has never traded can lose the job when the table disappears.
- Dealers pick up the donor's crops and seeds, process complete batches in their table, gather output, and tend nearby farmland while mob griefing is enabled. Tables retain partial inventories during dealer work. Converted addicts walk near players during work hours and take overdose damage from buying and restocking. Their body sway increases as health falls. The donor's normal and zombie profession overlays render over the legacy base skins.

Harmonium remains gated by the balancing option. The donor calls the vanilla dyed-armor factory, which leaves Harmonium undyed because it is an item. 1.12's merchant window refreshes new tiers on reopening. Per-player gossip and Hero of the Village adjustments and idle trade previews remain open.
- Feeding an adult nitwit or unemployed villager consumes a muffin in survival and unlocks the hidden advancement. Ported its drying table and cannabis advancement chain, including the joint branch. The smoking tools report the actual consumed ingredient to 1.12's consume-item criterion.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed 83 tests. Two new tests cover exact batch assembly across inventory slots, preserving surplus ingredients, and refusal to change an occupied table or move an incomplete batch.
- The packaged survival run checked native villager and zombie spawn assignments. A nearby unemployed adult claimed a table, picked up nine dropped leaves, loaded a drying batch, and collected three dried leaves. It harvested mature cannabis and planted tobacco. The first run exposed that Forge's public offer event returns a defensive copy of the list. Updating the villager's stored list fixed career conversion, tier unlocks and restocks while retaining the native trade event.
- A native merchant-window purchase crossed the seeded 10 XP threshold. A later workstation restock reset sold-out stock and raised its price based on demand. A trade at the seeded expert threshold added the configured Harmonium offer and the two-input LSA square offer. Feeding a nitwit consumed one muffin and awarded the advancement. A native addict trade paid emeralds and applied the expected overdose damage. The client showed the localized titles and profession clothes. Addict restocking and the zombie overlays still need runtime checks.
- A fresh Forge process reopened the same world and restored both professions, the dealer's expert stock and XP, the addict's used offer and the drying-table reservation.

### Cycle 32 · Rift Jar, Reality Rift and Glitch

- Ported the jar recipe, five creative variants, fill tooltip, sounds and both interactions. Regular use opens or closes the lid. Sneak-use switches between capturing nearby rifts and discharging Zero matter. The tile saves the fill, lid and mode, sends updates to tracking clients, and carries the exact fill into the harvested item.
- Reality Rifts spawn on the donor's configurable per-player interval. They apply Power and Zero to nearby survival players. Critical rifts damage nearby living entities and replace terrain as their radius advances. Forge checks the advancing shell in loaded chunks to avoid recursively loading terrain. Jar overload feeds an existing rift or releases one before the explosion. The release position and the client glyph connections follow the fixes in `467d2720`.
- The client uses the donor jar textures, animated cork and handle, cracked overlay, rift rays, center texture and particles. The GLSL 1.20 program projects the eight Zero matter textures and fifteen animated portal layers onto the jar interior and rift rays. Glyph trails use the 1.12 enchantment font and curve around nearby rifts or rise from a discharging jar.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed 93 tests. New tests cover the four jar facings, piston behavior, exact item fill and handling a nonfinite fill value.
- In installed Forge 14.23.5.2864, the fixture matched the jar recipe using a legacy plank variant, checked both creative settings, and spawned a random rift after the player entered loaded terrain. The placed jar captured from a tracked rift. Both the rift and the discharging jar applied Power and Zero to the survival player, and the creative player was excluded from rift exposure. Harvesting returned exactly one jar with the remaining `0.53772485` fill. Replacing it preserved that amount. A critical rift overloaded a jar, absorbed its fill and left no jar drop. A separate critical rift damaged a pig and changed stone to Glitch as the radius expanded. Right-click and landing callbacks cleared the Glitch blocks.
- The client received the jar fill and animation state, played the opening and mode-switch sounds, and resolved the item model and tooltip. The capture and discharge screenshots show the textured jar, rift rays and animated glyphs. A second Forge process reopened the save with the jar at exactly `0.77425`, in discharge mode, and the rift still present. The owner client received the persisted fill.
- A follow-up on a copy of the save confirmed the corrected escape position when an overloaded jar had no nearby rift. It also checked that a critical rift crossed the closing threshold, shrank to zero and expired. The screenshot shows the Glitch block alongside stone and the filled jar in hand and in the hotbar.
- The tested jar's SHA-256 is `96dec7d0c4072e774d1fb39a20dc60a1ca2ffc8c722a644b3cba2b4dde5d2fc2`. The logs and screenshots are under `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle32` in `runtime-capture.log`, `runtime-reopen.log`, `runtime-extras.log`, `game/screenshots` and `game-extras/screenshots`.

### Cycle 34 · Power overlay and motion blur

Forge keeps up to 30 samples at the legacy scaled screen size and captures on half-tick boundaries. Resource reload cleared the history, a Minecraft framebuffer resize rebuilt it at `475×272`, and turning motion blur off restored the original sample and deleted its textures.

### Cycle 35 · Sound gain and chat distortion

- Ported the donor's sound gain modifiers for Power, sleep deprivation and the LSD class shared by LSD and atropine. Forge applies the combined gain to new and already-playing channels without changing the saved category or master slider. A playing channel can mute and recover.
- Ported alcohol and kava slurring, Zero's increasingly corrupted text and binary, and cannabis filler with the donor's English and Polish translations. Independent Forge config settings control incoming and outgoing distortion. The client distorts incoming text before wrapping it, preserving the original in logs and chat history and keeping styles and interactive actions. Outgoing chat passes through the Forge event and remains within the legacy packet length. Slash commands bypass distortion.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed 99 tests. The added tests cover the LSD gain ramp and gain stacking, Zero's binary transformation with formatting and Unicode, and the outgoing chat length and command boundaries.
- Installed Forge 14.23.5.2864 opened OpenAL's `No Output` device. It measured block-category gains of `0.5` initially, `0.25` for existing and new sources under half-strength Power, `0` for both retained sources at full Power, and `0.75` under sleep deprivation. Both channels recovered to `0.5`, and the master slider stayed at `0.8`. These are sound-engine measurements. The fixture verified incoming Zero binary with color, click and hover actions, the original in raw history, outgoing binary at the integrated server, and an intact `/help` command. Turning both settings off restored plain input, output and rewrapped history. The first fixture used `/list`, which the integrated server did not register.
- Repeated the world-entry test on a fresh default-terrain world with seed `1668388832989348196`. The client rendered 12,310 frames and ran 2,109 ticks in 105 seconds, with a longest frame gap of 289 ms. The integrated server ran 2,131 ticks and completed 576 biome decorations. After the fixture requested shutdown and the server saved, 625 saved chunks contained 82 mod plant blocks across five species. The first-frame freeze did not reproduce. The reported session remains unconfirmed.
- The isolated client options template under `run/production/client` sets the master volume to zero. The muted Docker runner described in Cycle 48 uses OpenAL Soft's no-output backend, so sound-specific checks can change the fixture's sliders without playing audio.
- The tested jar's SHA-256 is `8afb5e661e4bac81eca1c905fdd0e30da5c204b7eb4e6656ec1a8d32cda78c3f`. Local evidence is under `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle35` in `runtime-silent.log`, `runtime-world-entry-normal.log` and `saved-normal-crops.log`.

### Cycle 36 · Background drug music

- Ported the donor's single ambient theme for the strongest enabled drug. The loop follows the player and scales from activation above `0.01` to full gain at `0.4`. A resource pack can provide `psychedelicraft:drug.<id>`. The stock jar uses the original streamed generic track when no theme is available. Clearing the effects or disconnecting releases the track.
- Forge's Mod Options exposes all 18 independent switches under Drug Theme Music. Each defaults to enabled and persists in the client's `config/psychedelicraft-client.cfg`.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed 101 tests. The added tests cover activation, tied and excluded drugs, and the gain ramp.
- Installed Forge 14.23.5.2864 opened OpenAL's `No Output` device. The Forge menu saved the caffeine switch in both positions and kept alcohol enabled. With caffeine disabled, the generic ambient loop held one source at gain `0.125` and followed the player's position. Enabling caffeine switched to the resource pack's streamed theme at gain `0.5`. Removing the pack during resource reload restored the generic loop. Muting and restoring the ambient slider stopped and restarted that source, clearing effects removed it, and the client returned to the menu with no active drug music.
- The first client stalled during startup before world creation. The native sample caught the client waiting at a JVM safepoint after the thread-dump request, which did not finish. The retry used `-XX:+UseSerialGC` and passed. The startup stall and the earlier reported first-frame freeze remain unexplained.
- The tested jar's SHA-256 is `538739c9eb9157c2b215313a036b34e9593927906a4ceb76515586d5d3bf5266`. Local evidence is in `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle36/runtime-retry.log`, `game-retry/screenshots/drug-theme-music.png` under the same directory, and `native-first.txt`.

### Cycle 37 · Reggie, identity swaps and hallucination commands

The head initialized at the requested block center `(-0.5, 7.5, 5.5)` and changed 6,872 of 16,384 sampled pixels. A real incoming chat packet prompted Reggie's local reply. The integrated server recorded zero outbound player chat events and retained 20 health. Both swaps expired and disconnect cleared the remaining contextual state.

### Cycle 38 · Single and grouped apparitions

- Ported the donor's drift, rotation, tint, scale and fade. Grouped apparitions draw 5–15 independently offset copies. Both types support command positions and random spawns near the player. They render against world depth and stay outside the world's tracked entities.

The ten later species have render-only voxel models with species-specific shapes and palettes. Eligible strong attempts can pick either swap after the head and group rolls, and weaker attempts fall back to a single apparition.

The command-rendered Enderman changed 5,530 sampled pixels and left the same sampled region unchanged for 13 frames behind a stone wall. The grouped command drew eight offset dolphins and changed 12,203 sampled pixels. The native Enderman never entered the world entity table. Individual screenshots of the other render-only models remain open.

The normal client tick produced a natural Enderman within range and one Reggie head without another command. Expiry and disconnect cleared the state. The first run timed out while waiting for a contextual slot. An intermediate run unloaded the world while vanilla packets were queued, and the final run closed the connection first and drained those packets.

### Cycle 39 · Camera drift, inertial input and poses

The player model probe measured `0.8573` radians of head tilt, matching the combined alcohol and kava dose, and verified copied clothing angles and restoration after the draw.

### Cycle 40 · Terrain geometry hallucinations

- Ported the pinned donor's `shaders/geometry/basic.gvsh` and `basic.gfsh` terrain effects to GLSL 1.20. The three wave formulas, distance deformation, LSD fracture, portal atlas sampling and mind-color pulses use the existing client effect strengths and player age. The shader uses the chunk's vertex coordinates, Forge's block atlas and lightmap, and the current fog mode.
- The Forge hook binds the shader around the original chunk layer draw and restores the previous program. Vanilla owns the surrounding alpha, blend, depth and texture state. The **Use 3D Shaders** setting controls the geometry shader independently of 2D effects. An inactive effect or shader load failure uses the existing chunk draw.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed 109 tests. The pulse test verifies that the weighted effect uses the mind palette, caps opacity at one and clears on expiry.
- On installed Forge 14.23.5.2864 with Minecraft's master volume at zero and OpenAL's silent output, both the VBO and display-list renderers bound the program for all four block layers and restored the previous binding after drawing. Each isolated effect changed at least 35,548 pixels by more than seven RGB levels in one channel. Stacking wiggle and portal tint changed more than 235,000 pixels on each renderer. Turning off 3D shaders with effects present, and clearing those effects with shaders enabled, each restored the original draw and returned the frame to within seven RGB levels per channel. Resource reload released the cached shader and the next affected draw rebuilt it.
- The fixture held the camera still and froze the water texture only for the underwater comparison. A near-zero wiggle enabled the shader while preserving the scene to within seven RGB levels per channel under both outdoor linear and underwater exponential fog. Full waves underwater changed 282,355 pixels beyond that tolerance. The client and integrated server shut down cleanly.
- The tested jar's SHA-256 is `a9f0634497c58a591024d61f10c8da249e2592dafd1fde474e9da12d5f5d4c91`. Local evidence is in `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle40/runtime-verified.log` and the captures under `game-verified/screenshots`. Other graphics drivers and the terrain effects combined with 2D post-processing remain open for runtime coverage.

Xvfb and Mesa software rendering keep the client window inside the Linux container, including the fullscreen switch and the return to 854 × 480. The launcher sets Minecraft's master volume to zero and the log confirms OpenAL's `No Output` device. Evidence is in `runtime-background.log` and `game-background/screenshots` in the same fixture directory.
- The muted Docker run also passed the command-driven Power and Zero regression with stacked effects, resource reload, framebuffer resize and cleanup. The fixture log is `runtime-background-digital.log`.
- The environmental fixture passed heat, water and coffee distortion, droplets, injury, heartbeat, Peyote, Harmonium stacking, reload and resize, agave, and rain with and without cover. It observed heartbeat and breathing requests before volume filtering, which allowed the test to keep master volume at zero. The fixture log is `runtime-background-environment-muted.log`.

Use the muted Docker runner under `run/production/background-qa` for automated client testing on this workstation. It uses the installed Forge client libraries and Gradle's Minecraft assets. Xvfb contains the game window, including fullscreen, and Minecraft's master volume and OpenAL's output are disabled. Keep native macOS launches for explicitly requested interactive tests so automated runs leave desktop focus alone. Build the runner image once, then invoke it from the repository root with the fixture directory, game directory name, save directory name, production jar, fixture jar, fixture mode, and log filename.

```sh
docker build --platform linux/amd64 -t psychedelicraft-forge-qa:java8 run/production/background-qa
qa=run/production/runtime-qa-cycle48
sh run/production/background-qa/run.sh "$qa" game-background Overlay-QA \
  build/libs/psychedelicraft-1.12.2-port.1.jar "$qa/build/libs/port-overlay-qa.jar" \
  overlays runtime-background.log
```

### Cycle 53 · Honey acquisition and mead

Shears yield three combs and take one durability. An empty glass bottle collects 125 mB. The mod’s replacement has custom nest and comb textures. The nest simulates pollination directly. Independent bee behavior and campfire smoke are separate vanilla mechanics.

The client drew distinct empty and full nest models.

### Cycle 54 · Waterlogged lattices

- The donor's `block/LatticeBlock` stores still water and copies it when a vine grows onto a lattice. Forge stores one 1,000 mB source in a tile entity for plain, morning glory and grape lattices. The four connection bits stay in the existing metadata layout. Saved crop tiles without a water field load dry. Crop growth and scheduled water flow use separate callbacks.
- Placement into a source, player buckets, dispensers and the Forge fluid capability fill and drain one source. Simulated operations preserve state. Breaking a wet lattice releases the source. The vanilla water tick calculates outflow and reads contained water when updating nearby currents and mixing with lava. Plain lattices retain piston movement and arrive dry, following the donor piston finalization. Cycle 99 extends that behavior to crop lattices and preserves their growth state.
- The native chunk renderer draws the lattice in its cutout layer and adds water in the translucent layer. Water height, the camera's fluid state, player immersion and the mod's underwater effects can see the contained source. Wet lattices suppress flammability.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed 120 tests. The muted packaged client exercised source placement, bucket and dispenser exchanges, simulated and partial transfers, wet crop conversion, persistent outflow, adjacent lava hardening, source release, piston movement and water detection. The sampled dry and wet lattices each emitted 48 cutout vertices. Only the wet lattice emitted translucent geometry. A second process reopened the save and verified both plain and crop sources, crop age, and the render layers again.
- The previous lattice fixture also passed its eight crafting, growth, shearing, pick, Silk Touch, model and crop-decay checkpoints during this cycle. Earlier runs exposed the need for explicit dispenser pickup support and a fixture camera position that includes the vanilla view offset.

### Cycle 74 · Sustained cardiac overdose

- The pinned donor's `SimpleDrug` advances each effect's active counter and checks its own heartbeat speed before decay. Forge now applies the same strict threshold above 3.0 on the server. The named damage source bypasses armor and leaves native protection in place. The effect resets its desired strength and sends a snapshot when unlocked. Locked effects keep their strength. English and Polish include the donor's death message.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed 139 tests. The packaged jar's SHA-256 is `b37a39a0f8ae0241c0dc8199bc9be149018727c56c0bf943711319b216950d1c`. The build log is `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle74/build-production.log`.
- In muted Forge 14.23.5.2864 on Docker's Xvfb display, the fixture restored a saved caffeine effect one tick before its heartbeat reached exactly 3.0. That tick caused no damage. On the next tick a separate creative check retained full health and reset the unlocked effect. Sugar stayed safe just below the threshold, then triggered native damage above it. The totem was consumed, the player survived at one health, and the unlocked effect reset. The fixture cleared the remaining active strength to isolate the next scenario.
- Sustained locked caffeine killed the player through a diamond chestplate and produced the English death message. The integrated server and owner client both cleared the effect on native respawn. The fixture seeded NBT near the thresholds to exercise the production tick path without waiting several minutes per dose. The passing log is `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle74/runtime-cardiac-threshold.log`. It confirms OpenAL used `No Output` and the world saved on shutdown.

### Cycle 75 · Stimulant movements and fatigue exhaustion

- Ported the pinned `DrugProperties` rolls for caffeine, sugar, cocaine and bath salts. The client adds each effect's thresholded chances. It uses the native jump for grounded players and the main-hand swing when an animation can start. The server applies 0.05 exhaustion per successful drowsiness roll through the native food system, using sleep deprivation after stimulant energy is subtracted.
- The eyelid overlay now reads the same drowsiness modifier as exhaustion. The pinned `DrugType` registers kava with its alcohol profile, which has no drowsiness contribution. This corrects the extra kava contribution introduced in Cycle 48.
- The Java 8 clean build and reobfuscation passed 141 tests, including the chance thresholds and their sums, the registered kava profile, and stimulant masking of sleep deprivation.
- In muted Forge 14.23.5.2864, a disposable world fixture seeded effect snapshots and fixed selected random rolls. The owner jumped from the ground and started a main-hand swing at a roll above any single effect's chance. The next airborne tick neither jumped again nor restarted the swing. The integrated server received both through Minecraft's movement and animation packets.
- Eight server ticks at full drowsiness added 0.40 exhaustion. The creative player and the player whose fatigue was masked by caffeine gained none. Kava alone left exhaustion unchanged. Kava with subthreshold fatigue did not start a blink, and full sleep deprivation started the overlay. The last two checks inspected the live overlay state. The fixture did not compare rendered pixels or remote observer animation.
- The packaged jar's SHA-256 is `cab72f0607e4026794695e27d6f9a34c043b82f49f8dad244b56bf25162c81c1`. ### Cycle 78 · Tray overhang selection

- The pinned `TrayBlock` extends one pixel beyond its own cell at both ends. Its `MixinVoxelShape` leaves the hit assigned to the tray for an overhang this small. The previous Forge jar matched the shape in a block-local ray test, but world rays through all four overhangs missed the tray on both sides of an integrated game.
- Forge now checks loaded adjacent trays along the normal world ray traversal. It tests the tray's existing oriented shape and retains the owning block position. A nearer native block hit still wins. The first implementation hit a read-only ASM frame list during launch. The working transformer copies the list before adding the candidate local.
- The Java 8 clean build and reobfuscation passed, and the suite passed 142 tests after adding an assertion for the owning position, hit face, and ordering against nearer and farther block hits. The new jar SHA-256 is `f191e2778e366a3bfcafc7f26b635057bd8d8d4981f881a70292079fa2e912b0`.
- The packaged jar passed world rays through the center, all four overhangs, and the air just outside them. It checked side hits, a lip at a chunk boundary, a stone block that occluded the lip, and both bounding-box and return-last-miss flags. The native client crosshair selected the lip, and its creative break packet removed the tray on the server while preserving neighboring stone. The first gameplay fixture passed the ray checks but waited for an exact player height after the player had already landed on the tray. The passing fixture waits for the reported horizontal position and aim.
- The same jar passed the native bucket and dispenser regression for tagged fluid placement and recovery, flow, lava mixing, and Nether evaporation. All client runs used the muted Docker display. Logs are under `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle78` in `runtime-before.log`, `runtime-verified.log`, `runtime-fluid-regression.log`, `build-production-final.log`, and `test-overhang.log`.

### Cycle 86 · Reproduce the single-player disconnect stall

- The fixture opened fresh creative flat worlds, spawned eight tracked pigs and clicked the native Save and Quit to Title button through `GuiScreen.mouseClicked`. It varied the pause-menu dwell from one to three client ticks and checked for a saved world, stopped server and returned title screen. Both configurations used the same fixture, Forge 14.23.5.2864, Java 8, Docker's Xvfb display, zero master volume and OpenAL's `No Output` device.
- Stock Forge returned on all 18 clicks across three clients. With the Cycle 85 production jar, 10 of 12 clicks returned across three clients. Two clients stalled after the integrated server saved and unloaded the world. In each thread dump, the client was parked in `Futures.getUnchecked` inside `IntegratedServer.initiateShutdown`, called by `Minecraft.loadWorld` from the native menu. The server thread was absent from both dumps. The test containers were stopped after collecting the dumps.
- Late vanilla entity packet exceptions also occurred after a successful title return in each configuration. The packet exceptions and the stalled shutdown have separate evidence. Source inspection shows the server can start shutting down when the owner logs out while the client is also requesting shutdown. The client can queue a cleanup task after the server's final task drain. This is consistent with the captured wait. These runs reproduce the stall with the packaged port and leave its frequency on stock Forge unmeasured beyond the sample.
- Evidence and the reproducible runner are under `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle86`. The stock logs are `runtime-stock.log`, `runtime-stock-repeat.log` and `runtime-stock-third.log`. The packaged runs are `runtime-mod.log`, `runtime-mod-repeat.log` and `runtime-mod-third.log`. `run-disconnect.sh` starts either configuration. The production jar tested is the Cycle 85 jar, SHA-256 `59514c451558bba832029d8d694c26d76f9b16c11d971f2185e66c8ceb24e3f7`.

A completed task's result or failure still propagates.

### Cycle 91 · Hero of the Village in survival

- The pinned mod uses vanilla price handling, and its custom villagers have no active Hero gift task. Forge 1.12 uses its native zombie siege as the survival equivalent of the newer raid. A player who kills a siege attacker can earn rank I for 48,000 ticks once the wave has finished, all tracked attackers have died and a villager survives. Rank I corresponds to a single native siege. Higher command-granted ranks retain the donor's price formula.
- The Forge siege setup event creates a record and a scoped hook marks only successfully spawned native siege attackers. A regular zombie contributes no credit. Interrupted spawning and a village with no survivors give no reward. The saved data keeps defender and attacker UUIDs once spawning is complete. In-progress spawning is transient in vanilla and an interrupted wave cannot grant victory after a restart.
- The Java 8 production build and reobfuscation passed 150 tests. The added test checks the saved wave, both participant sets, and removal of interrupted spawning records. The rebuilt archive has the same 1,301 entry contents as the first tested jar.
- The muted Forge 14.23.5.2864 fixture supplied a village with ten doors and twenty residents and selected the native siege state to avoid waiting for the random midnight roll. Native spawn attempts produced 12 attackers, all tracked. A player kill early in the wave earned credit, and the final kill by another source awarded the effect. The dealer quote dropped from six emeralds to five and the client displayed the emerald icon. The previous jar defeated a native wave without receiving the effect. Separate runs verified the interrupted siege and loss of all residents. The initial fixture expected every native spawn attempt to succeed. The corrected fixture checks completed spawning and counts the successful attempts.
- The save fixture left one tagged attacker alive after spawning completed. A fresh process recovered the attacker and the original defender's credit. Defeating that attacker through a non-player damage source awarded the original defender the same effect and quote. The first reopen probe counted all join callbacks. The successful retry looked for the living tagged attacker in the loaded world.
- The built jar SHA-256 is `4bd52b1c9482e55c5a334b2f8704660e7a023127bc4bbf2837a8524a8e117299`. Evidence is in `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle91` under `build-production-final.log`, `build-tests-final.log`, `runtime-baseline-final.log`, `runtime-fixed-final.log`, `runtime-abort.log`, `runtime-loss.log`, `runtime-save.log`, `runtime-reopen-retry.log` and `runtime-capture.log`. The HUD capture is `game-capture/screenshots/hero-of-the-village.png`.

### Cycle 92 · Cocaine desaturation

- The pinned `CocaineDrug` contributes desaturation at 0.75 times its active value, independently of the random color hallucinations. The later reference branches retain that modifier. Restored it through Forge's existing `DrugProperties.getDesaturation` and screen shader. The active donor registration still uses the alcohol profile for kava, so the unused `KavaDrug` desaturation is outside this port.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed 151 tests. The added regression checks the zero baseline, a 0.4 active cocaine value, and blending with tobacco and a random hallucination through their remaining color fractions.
- Muted Forge 14.23.5.2864 clients received the test values over the normal server-to-client packet. With bloom active, the same color sample was `(255, 105, 40)` at zero desaturation on the previous jar and `(243, 114, 63)` at 0.30 on the rebuilt jar. Adding tobacco produced 0.37 and `(230, 117, 70)`. The fixture models both bloom passes and the desaturation calculation. An early probe compared against the desaturation calculation alone and exposed the need to account for bloom.
- Turning off 2D effects restored the input color. Reloading resources and switching to 1920 × 1080 fullscreen restored the stacked result. Clearing both effects and returning to 854 × 480 windowed restored the same input color. The fixture verified that the draw restored the active shader program and reported no OpenGL errors.
- The same jar passed the 17-stage overlay regression for warmth, alcohol, eyelids, injury, Power, portal tint, pulses, F1, resource reload, fullscreen and sun occlusion. The tested jar SHA-256 is `49a9f1d44a4cce5a82dfbc734b4bcade7bbd916df1af606b43238325ce7baa4e`. The isolated clients used Xvfb and OpenAL's `No Output` device. Evidence is under `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle92` in `build-production.log`, `runtime-baseline-final.log`, `runtime-fixed-final.log` and `runtime-overlay.log`.

With wiggle waves and surface fractals active, it observed the shader on every part of the allay, bee, axolotl, camel, dolphin, warden, glow squid, cod, salmon, tropical fish and Reggie. The previous jar used the fixed-function path for all eleven. Each model changed the framebuffer on the rebuilt jar. Depth writes stayed off, Reggie retained his texture, and the shader program and active texture unit were restored without OpenGL errors.
- The allay drew without the shader when 3D effects were disabled. After enabling the setting before the world pass and reloading resources, all nine parts drew with a new shader instance. The first probe changed the setting after Forge captured the view matrix. The corrected probe changes it before rendering starts and samples each model's contribution to the framebuffer around the actual contextual draw.

### Cycle 97 · Explosion drop-decay game rules

- The pinned mod uses 1.20.1's three explosion drop-decay rules. The native explosion selects the rule at construction and passes the selected chance through the existing block drop callback. The hook keeps Forge's destruction, harvest, and tile-drop behavior in place.
- Primed TNT and TNT minecarts use the TNT rule. Creepers, withers, their projectiles and the mod's explosive projectile use the mob rule. Beds, end crystals, rift jars and other source-less blasts use the block rule. A native large fireball supplies its identity for the rule while keeping the null explosion source used by 1.12's damage and Forge callbacks.

The native fireball impact ran 24 times with block decay on and mob decay off, and yielded all 48 leaves while Forge still reported a null explosive owner. The first fireball probe expected air after removal and encountered fire. The retry checked that the crop was gone and counted its dropped leaves.
- A fresh Forge process reopened the saved world, recovered all three typed overrides, and repeated 24 drop trials for each rule. ### Cycle 106 · Juniper door support and slab loot

- The pinned `DoorBlock` checks the full upper face below the lower half. Forge now uses the same support check for placement and retention. Both halves detach when the support is lost. The lower half supplies the one door item. Creative removal of either half removes both halves without loot. The legacy two-block metadata, hinge and model stay in use.
- The door now compares the signal at both halves with its saved powered state when a neighbor updates. The native door toggle handles the open state and sound. A manual closure while powered remains until the next power edge.
- Both later branches replace the pinned slab's one-item loot table with vanilla `slabDrops`. Forge's native drop callback already emits two individual stacks for a double slab and rolls the blast chance separately for each. Added regression tests for those two stacks and for the lower-half door drop. The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed all 157 tests.
- In muted Forge 14.23.5.2864, the previous jar lost doors placed on glass or closed top trapdoors and kept the door open after a relayed signal disappeared and the stone neighbor notified it. The rebuilt jar held on both supports, dropped exactly once when they were removed or opened, and closed when the signal disappeared. The first relay probe used a redstone block that did not strongly power through the stone. The passing probe used a supported repeater and changed its facing without sending a separate repeater removal notification. The rebuilt jar reported a successful harvest and returned one item from either half in survival, with no item from either half in creative.
- Of 120 radius-two double-slab destruction trials, 27 dropped zero slabs, 57 dropped one and 36 dropped two. Default TNT dropped both. Of 64 trials striking the lower door half, 30 dropped the door. All 64 upper-half-only strikes dropped the door through the lower half's support loss. The packaged building regression also passed slab merging and harvest, door interaction, the eight recipes and client models.
- The packaged jar SHA-256 is `a1ae0960d1f5d040b5e2ab44de6c0312c15402f60529911ca28ccaec39d82ded`. Focused evidence is under `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle106` in `build-production-verified.log`, `build-fixture-turned.log`, `runtime-baseline-turned.log` and `runtime-fixed-turned.log`. The content-identical building regression log is `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle9/runtime-cycle106.log`. Clients used Xvfb and OpenAL's `No Output` device at zero master volume.

### Cycle 107 · Potted plant support and interactions

- The pinned `BlockConstructionUtils.pottedPlant` registers ordinary 1.20.1 `FlowerPotBlock` instances. The donor inherits the unconditional survival check, so all seven filled Psychedelicraft pots now stay in place when the block underneath is removed. The empty pot continues to use Forge 1.12's native block and tile entity.
- Clicking a modded filled pot with a pottable item keeps its current plant. An empty hand takes the contents, and a held non-pottable item sends them to inventory or to the ground if inventory is full. Forge uses the seven registered mod ingredients and the legacy flower pot's accepted items, including fern metadata. The empty-pot insertion hook respects Forge's explicit block-use allowance for sneaking.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed all 157 tests. On muted Forge 14.23.5.2864, the previous jar emptied the filled pot when clicked with coffee cherries, a juniper sapling, a dandelion or a fern. The rebuilt jar kept the pot intact for all four, returned the plant to inventory when clicked with tall grass, and dropped nothing when support was removed. The probe filled and emptied all seven plants with the off hand, checked pick-block, verified that creative filling kept the held item, and confirmed that a piston dropped both components once.
- The authored loot tables use separate explosion rolls for the pot and plant. Forge already rolls the chance per stack. The pinned snapshot lacks the morning glory pot table. Both later branches generate the same two-pool table as the other six pots, matching the port. In 80 radius-two destruction trials on the rebuilt jar, 16 dropped nothing, 20 dropped only the pot, 21 dropped only the plant and 23 dropped both.
- A fresh Forge process reopened the same save, found the unsupported pot intact and returned the juniper sapling to the off hand. The tested jar SHA-256 is `6130cdd850906d03dc5a9cc3702ed9648469784ec117749ebe277ea74e360f27`. Evidence is under `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle107` in `build-production.log`, `build-fixture.log`, `runtime-baseline.log`, `runtime-fixed.log` and `runtime-reopen.log`. Clients ran on Xvfb with OpenAL's `No Output` device and zero master volume.
- The juniper regression baked all seven pot models, grew a sapling, checked leaf and wood behavior, and synchronized pot insertion and removal through the client controller. Directly breaking the suspended pot dropped both components. The first regression timed out while waiting for the server to accept a click after the fixture teleported the player. The passing fixture gave the player a floor at the interaction point and rendered a grown tree and filled pot for 40 frames. The log is `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle107-regression/runtime-regression-final.log`.

### Cycle 108 · Juniper sign attachments

- The donor's `StandingAndWallBlockItem` considers the clicked face and then the player's look directions. Forge now tries the available standing and wall placements in that order. Standing and ordinary wall signs recheck their respective support when that neighbor changes.
- The donor's `WallHangingSignBlock.canPlace` requires a full side face or an aligned wall hanging sign. Its inherited survival check allows an existing sign to stay after either side is removed. Forge now keeps those checks distinct and accepts the outer face of an open trapdoor. The port recognizes juniper hanging signs as compatible attachments, following the sign tags generated by both later branches.
- Ceiling signs require support at the center of the underside. Forge uses its face classifications and collision boxes as the legacy equivalent for support shapes. Leaves provide no support. Changing the ceiling can remove a chain of signs and restore any water source they contained.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed all 157 tests. In muted Forge 14.23.5.2864, the previous jar lost an isolated wall hanging sign, accepted a ceiling sign below leaves, and refused an underside click that could place an ordinary sign against a nearby wall. The rebuilt jar kept the isolated wall sign, refused leaves and found the wall. It also accepted an open trapdoor side, verified that a bottom slab used straight chains, and dropped one sign when the slab changed to an upper slab. Removing a wet chain's support returned two signs and restored the water source.
- A fresh Forge process reopened the unsupported wall hanging sign and recovered one item when it was broken. The hanging-sign regression verified the recipes, model baking, client placement and editor synchronization for five signs, operator-authored text and rendering of both chain forms and the beam. The regression log is `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle108-regression/runtime-regression.log`.
- The ordinary sign regression passed standing and wall placement, both editable faces, Forge placement cancellation, wax, dye, glow and rendering. The initial run timed out opening the back editor after teleporting. The passing fixture used a four-chunk render distance, a 30 FPS cap and disabled mob spawning. The log is `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle108-regular/runtime-regression.log`.
- The tested jar SHA-256 is `c3131aab2af285bf02f9b07e3402a00043853edde72faeee3d3a0b964c4abb81`. Focused evidence is under `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle108` in `build-production.log`, `build-fixture.log`, `runtime-baseline.log`, `runtime-fixed.log` and `runtime-reopen.log`.
