### Cycle 111 · Juniper boat launches

- The pinned `PSItems` delegates both hulls to Terraform Wood API 7.0.0-beta.1. Its hand ray accepts source and flowing fluids and spawns at the closest outline or fluid hit. Forge now compares the native outline ray with the fluid shapes along the same five-block segment. Vanilla and mod liquids use their level-based surface, with a full-height column when the same fluid continues above. The launch retains the player's facing, hull collision check and server-side item consumption after a successful spawn. [Pinned Terraform sources](https://maven.terraformersmc.com/releases/com/terraformersmc/terraform-api/terraform-wood-api-v1/7.0.0-beta.1/terraform-wood-api-v1-7.0.0-beta.1-sources.jar)
- Dispensers use the port's donor water-tag equivalent. Water-tagged fluids and waterlogged blocks qualify in front of the dispenser or below an air block in front. The Terraform offsets of 1.125 blocks along the dispenser face and one extra block above a fluid in front carry over. Untagged fluid keeps the dispenser's item fallback.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed all 158 tests. In muted Forge 14.23.5.2864, the previous jar passed through flowing water, coffee and agave and ejected boat items at the three tagged-fluid or waterlogged dispenser positions. The rebuilt jar placed both hulls at the expected fluid heights and launched them at those dispenser positions. The fixture also verified stone occlusion, overlapping-bank refusal, item retention on a canceled Forge spawn and the untagged agave dispenser fallback.
- A 12-block drop onto stone left both hulls intact because Forge cleared their fall distance on first contact. The fixture then set a four-block fall distance on the landed boats and exercised the next native tick. Each hull dropped three juniper planks and two sticks, and the chest boat returned all 19 carrots exactly once. The chest-loot regression also passed spectator refusal, seeded generation, shift-click, drop rules, native Nether transfer and slot replacement.
- The boat regression passed both survival recipes, a real client launch, two-rider rowing, chest-boat dispensing and automation, mounted inventory access and the one-rider limit. Its first run timed out at the screenshot position after teleporting the player above an unsupported block. With a solid platform at that point, the rebuilt fixture captured both hulls in the water and the open cargo screen. The passing log is `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle111/runtime-boat-regression.log`.

### Cycle 112 · Boat flotation in tagged liquids

- The pinned boat code compares the hull's bottom and top against the fluid surface, distinguishes submerged sources from flowing water, and adds world Y after the upward scan finds a fractional surface. Forge 1.12's fluid hooks added world Y inside that scan and treated any hull contact in the top block as flowing submersion. The port now shares the level-based liquid height with boat placement and checks the actual top of the hull. Waterlogged blocks supply source water. The same fluid in the block above fills the lower cell to its top. [1.20.1 boat mappings](https://maven.fabricmc.net/docs/yarn-1.20.1%2Bbuild.10/net/minecraft/entity/vehicle/BoatEntity.html)
- The three fluid checks feed Forge's existing movement and steering for vanilla and juniper hulls. Other Forge blocks can continue to report water contact through their existing material and height hooks. The fall-distance check recognizes the donor water tag below the boat. Agave remains outside that tag.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed all 160 tests, including stacked fractional fluid heights and source-versus-flowing submersion. In muted Forge 14.23.5.2864, the previous jar returned a three-block height for the stacked coffee fixture. The rebuilt jar returned `1 + 6/9` blocks. It also classified partial immersion as floating, distinguished submerged sources from flowing blocks and treated a waterlogged lattice as a source.
- After 100 native entity ticks, the vanilla boat and juniper chest boat in source coffee settled at Y `24.523264`, matching the juniper boat in water. Both coffee hulls settled about `0.111111` blocks too high on the previous jar. Agave left the hull on the basin floor. The launch regression passed hand placement, dispenser positioning, spawn cancellation and Forge's hard-land drop path.
- The packaged jar also passed the boat and chest-loot regressions for rowing, both rendered hulls, mounted inventory, shift-click, seeded loot, cargo drops and native Nether transfer. Their logs are `runtime-boat-regression.log` and `runtime-chest-regression.log` in the same fixture directory.
- The tested production jar SHA-256 is `41cebf6b50c7823d3fcc28b1baec8302b9c79f8aa6e5cd8a4aadacdea5d2e00a`. Evidence is under `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle112` in `build-production-final.log`, `runtime-baseline-final.log`, `runtime-fixed-final.log` and `runtime-launch-regression.log`. Clients ran on Xvfb with OpenAL's `No Output` device and zero master volume.

### Cycle 113 · Bubble columns and boats

- Backported the source-water columns that the donor's native boats inherit. Soul sand creates upward columns and magma creates downward columns. A gap or flowing cell stops propagation. Removing the supporting block restores ordinary source water. The mod block stores direction in metadata and uses the native liquid renderer and biome water tint. It can be bucketed and supplies water to waterloggable blocks. The legacy magma tick now forms a column in source water in place of evaporating it. [1.20.1 bubble column mappings](https://maven.fabricmc.net/docs/yarn-1.20.1%2Bbuild.10/net/minecraft/block/BubbleColumnBlock.html)
- Vanilla and juniper hulls count 60 ticks of continuous surface contact. An upward column launches an empty hull at `0.6` blocks per tick or a hull with a player at `2.7`. A downward column subtracts `0.7` from vertical velocity and ejects passengers. Submerged bodies receive the donor's current acceleration, and a living entity whose eyes are in a column skips the legacy drowning branch. The native entity tracker sends the surface timer and the launch velocity. Both render passes use the interpolated roll. Ambient visuals and sounds use the 1.12 bubble and wake sprites and water and lava ambience.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed all 162 tests, including direction metadata and the different motion limits inside a column and at its surface. The previous packaged jar did not form either column. In muted Forge 14.23.5.2864 the rebuilt jar formed both three-cell columns, left flowing water outside the column, slid the chest hull downward, ejected its pig without spilling any of its 19 carrots, lifted the empty juniper hull and canceled a charge when the boat left the surface. The controlling client rose while still mounted, and the client observed a synchronized roll angle of `2.68°`. Removing magma restored all three source cells.
- Initial packaged runs exposed a renderer stack-frame error, a breathing hook descriptor mismatch and a missing soul-sand scheduled callback. The passing jar uses the renderer's method entry, the runtime living-entity descriptor and the existing scheduled block dispatcher. The test jar SHA-256 is `4f8c904a1325c4779165da39710a9eeb68267cd404e0f88845563358ef088120`. Evidence is under `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle113` in `build-production-final.log`, `runtime-baseline.log` and `runtime-fixed.log`. Clients ran on Xvfb with OpenAL's `No Output` device and zero master volume.
- The expanded run verified a matching water tint on the client, air recovery through the native living-entity update, and pickup of one column cell with a vanilla bucket. A fresh Forge process restored the soul-sand column and the ordinary water where magma had been removed. The buoyancy regression retested the water, coffee, agave and waterlogged lattice cases from cycle 112. These logs are `runtime-fixed-final.log`, `runtime-reopen-final.log` and `runtime-buoyancy-regression.log`.

### Cycle 114 · Juniper boat boarding and dismounts

- Juniper hulls now check the liquid surface at the boat's eye point when boarding. The check uses a point one ninth of a block below the top of the hull and respects the donor water tag and fluid levels. A chest boat that cannot accept a rider still opens its cargo. Normal boats retain two seats and chest boats retain one. [1.20.1 boat mappings](https://maven.fabricmc.net/docs/yarn-1.20.1%2Bbuild.10/net/minecraft/entity/vehicle/BoatEntity.html)
- The server dismounts living juniper-boat riders in the direction they face. It checks the collision heights at the upper and lower bank positions, tests standing and legacy crouching clearance for players, and checks the world border. With a wet or obstructed bank it places the rider above the hull center. Forge 1.12 has standing and crouching player sizes for this search. The dismount runs after Forge accepts the unmount and before the existing player-position packet.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed. The suite reached 164 passing tests after adding coverage for boarding at different liquid levels and for full-block, slab and wet-bank dismounts. The previous jar let riders mount the submerged fixtures and placed the chest boat rider at Y `12.7635` above a slab whose top was Y `12.5`.
- In muted Forge 14.23.5.2864 the rebuilt jar refused boarding in source water, tagged coffee and sufficiently high flowing water. It allowed boarding above shallow flow and in untagged agave. The submerged chest boat opened its inventory with all 19 carrots. The dismount probe placed the rider on the slab and used the hull-top fallback at obstructed and wet banks. The controlling player received the exact server position on a full-block dock. The first full-block probe exposed an erroneous second Y offset in the legacy collision-box conversion. The unit tests cover that conversion.

### Cycle 115 · Dealer worksite reservations

- Dealer worksite memory now records the Forge dimension with the block position, following the donor's dimension-scoped job site. Saved dealer memories that contain only coordinates pick up their current dimension when the entity loads. A dealer elsewhere retains the original site and trade experience. - Death and lightning conversion release the dealer's reservation. When the site is unloaded, a save-wide record keeps the original dimension, coordinates and dealer UUID until the table's chunk loads. The release matches the table's owner, preserving another dealer's reservation and the table inventory. Ordinary chunk unloading keeps active reservations.

The Nether table at the same coordinates kept its separate reservation. After a fresh launch both deferred releases applied, all three table inventories and the living dealer's reservation survived, and an unemployed villager claimed a released site through normal AI. ### Cycle 130 · Shallow currents and falling fluid

- World mod fluids use differences in their own heights to calculate flow vectors. A falling cell uses the donor's full flowing height and adds downward pull beside a solid face. Forge derives falling from a flowing cell fed from above, so a stacked source keeps its source current. The solid-face check excludes ice and reads both the adjacent face and the one above it. [Pinned physical fluid](https://github.com/Sollace/Psychedelicraft/blob/319a4ea0160d6600cffcd5f5cc9e363ceaab44a1/src/main/java/ivorius/psychedelicraft/fluid/physical/PlacedFluid.java)
- The mod-fluid push uses the donor's depth scaling below 0.4 blocks, averages contributing cells, and normalizes the result for nonplayers. A player nearly at rest receives the donor's minimum push of `0.0045` when the flow produces a smaller nonzero push. The Forge hook combines water contact with the legacy result and applies the mod current separately from native water and other Forge fluids.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed all 173 tests. The new regression checks a horizontal source above a falling cell, free fall without a wall, downward pull next to stone and the ice exception. The packaged jar also passed the immersion regression for shallow contact, stacked flow, breathing and client eye checks.

### Cycle 131 · Currents across water and mod fluid boundaries

- The pinned game samples every contacted cell in the water tag before averaging and applying one push. When a water-tagged mod fluid touches an entity, Forge now includes vanilla water, waterlogged sources and bubble-column sources in that calculation. The surface check includes still water and excludes shallow fluid below the entity. Each fluid type still uses its own neighboring cells to determine its current. [Pinned water tag](https://github.com/Sollace/Psychedelicraft/blob/319a4ea0160d6600cffcd5f5cc9e363ceaab44a1/src/main/resources/data/minecraft/tags/fluids/water.json)
- Forge keeps its existing calculation for water-only movement, vat contact and third-party fluid callbacks. The combined push uses the entity's motion before legacy currents are applied, so the minimum player push is calculated once for the sampled water and mod cells.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed all 174 tests. The new regression covers exact cancellation before mob normalization, exclusion of a dry sample, and fallback to Forge for vanilla-only contact.
- In muted Forge 14.23.5.2864, opposing shallow water and coffee currents pushed a moving player `-0.0130029` blocks per tick on the previous jar and `0` on the rebuilt jar. The new result also canceled from rest and for a pig. A still water cell, waterlogged lattice or bubble column halved the shallow push. Water below the player's feet contributed nothing. At right angles the two pushes averaged to about `0.000499` and `-0.007` blocks per tick. The packaged jar also passed the separate native water, falling flow, immersion and breathing checks.

### Cycle 135 · Save every tick of fluid processing

- The Forge machines already incremented and serialized their timers, but the chunk was marked dirty only at the client update interval. After a save, an entity-free chunk could advance a vat's fermentation or hardening timer and then skip its next save. Every elapsed increment now marks the chunk dirty while client updates retain their 20-tick cadence. The shared change covers vats, barrels and distilleries. [Pinned processing timer](https://github.com/Sollace/Psychedelicraft/blob/319a4ea0160d6600cffcd5f5cc9e363ceaab44a1/src/main/java/ivorius/psychedelicraft/block/entity/FluidProcessingBlockEntity.java)
- The port retains the LTS corrections for acetification and for making dirt at exactly 4,000 mB of slurry. Successful processing retains batch attributes and any unconsumed slurry. [Pinned slurry processing](https://github.com/Sollace/Psychedelicraft/blob/319a4ea0160d6600cffcd5f5cc9e363ceaab44a1/src/main/java/ivorius/psychedelicraft/fluid/SlurryFluid.java)
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation succeeded. All 177 tests passed, including a regression that verifies advancing between client updates marks the chunk for saving.
- In muted Forge 14.23.5.2864, both jars advanced fermentation and slurry hardening to six ticks in memory. The previous jar's second save left both at one tick on disk. The rebuilt jar saved six ticks for both. Reading the region files and reopening each checkpoint in a fresh Forge process confirmed those values. The fixture copied the region immediately after the second save so shutdown activity could not change the sample. Both jars completed two fermentation stages and acetification without losing volume and produced one dirt with 500 mB of tagged slurry left over.
- The same jar passed barrel GUI transfer, maturation, tap and exact-capacity hardening, plus distillery heat, tagged transfer, outlet refusal and a disconnect/reconnect cycle. ### Cycle 136 · Distillery timing under changing heat

- The pinned distillery recalculates its required time from the heat below the block and increments elapsed time once per ready tick. Forge now uses that clock, including integer division, the minimum duration and the separate completion tick. Lava runs at rate seven, fire at three, magma keeps the LTS rate of two, and an unheated block runs at one. [Pinned distillery](https://github.com/Sollace/Psychedelicraft/blob/319a4ea0160d6600cffcd5f5cc9e363ceaab44a1/src/main/java/ivorius/psychedelicraft/block/entity/DistilleryBlockEntity.java) [Pinned timer](https://github.com/Sollace/Psychedelicraft/blob/319a4ea0160d6600cffcd5f5cc9e363ceaab44a1/src/main/java/ivorius/psychedelicraft/block/entity/FluidProcessingBlockEntity.java)
- A disconnected outlet clears elapsed progress as in the pinned game. A connected receiver that is full, locked or holds incompatible fluid still pauses the timer and leaves both tanks intact. The port retains that behavior to preserve the receiver transaction. Existing Forge saves convert active heat-weighted progress to the same fraction of the newly calculated duration on the first server tick. Converted saves record the new clock format.
- The clean Java 8 build and reobfuscation passed all 178 tests. The new regression covers duration rounding, the minimum and the completion tick for each heat rate.
- In muted Forge 14.23.5.2864, switching to lava after 20 unheated ticks completed the batch on the next ready tick and transferred 1 mB of slurry. The previous jar advanced to 27 heat-weighted ticks. Fire shortened the active duration and removing it restored the unheated duration. Removing the receiver cleared the new timer. A blocked receiver preserved 20 elapsed ticks through a save and reload even when lava lowered the required duration below 20, and the progress display capped at 100 percent. A legacy save at 50 of 100 ticks converted to 7 of 15 lava ticks, then advanced to 8.
- A fresh Forge process restored the blocked lava batch at eight elapsed ticks and completed it once in the remaining seven ticks after unblocking. ### Cycle 154 · Reality Rift collapse and reload

- The advancing shell now squares its float radii in double precision, matching the pinned distance comparison. In packaged Forge, the previous jar skipped a stone on the rounded outer boundary and converted a stone inside the previous radius. The rebuilt jar converted the outer stone and kept the inner stone. [Pinned Reality Rift tick](https://github.com/Sollace/Psychedelicraft/blob/319a4ea0160d6600cffcd5f5cc9e363ceaab44a1/src/main/java/ivorius/psychedelicraft/entity/RealityRiftEntity.java#L181)
- The native `/kill` command now starts an animated closure, following the later fix. The previous jar removed the entity in the command handler. NBT loading preserves initialized values for absent fields and reads explicit zero values. The zero random-spawn option continues to control random spawning, as in the pinned branch. [Animated command closure](https://github.com/Sollace/Psychedelicraft/commit/503822c1b242812400b424e5779250b6dc434b9f) · [Missing NBT defaults](https://github.com/Sollace/Psychedelicraft/commit/a452debc6d7b308199852b4f0e5519c3d3d1cd79)
- In Forge 14.23.5.2864, a Rift crossed from instability `0.8991` to `0.9001`, converted only the expected block at the three sampled radii, and kept size `3.25` on the transition tick. The next ticks shrank it by `0.05` without growing the shell. The client received the closing flag, size and instability. Counting the transition as tick 1, size reached zero on tick 67 and the entity was removed on tick 108. A region scan found the saved Rift UUID with size `1.3000019`, instability `0.9001` and the closing flag set. A fresh Forge process resumed from that state, preserved the shell, reached size zero on its measured tick 26, and removed the entity from server and client tracking after the visual tail on tick 55. Visual size is recomputed after reopening, as in the donor.
- The clean Java 8 build reobfuscated the production jar, and the subsequent suite passed all 187 tests. Two new tests cover command closure and missing and explicit NBT values. ### Cycle 155 · Rift Jar intake and overload order

- The jar now visits Rifts in the donor's section order, preserving the native list order within a section. This controls the order of float additions during intake and which Rift receives the jar on overload. The intake rate uses the donor's division and multiplication order, observable in a three-Rift unit regression with the lid 56 percent open. [Pinned Rift Jar tick and release](https://github.com/Sollace/Psychedelicraft/blob/319a4ea0160d6600cffcd5f5cc9e363ceaab44a1/src/main/java/ivorius/psychedelicraft/block/entity/RiftJarBlockEntity.java#L66)
- A fixture ran the actual Minecraft 1.20.1 section query with Rift-sized boxes. Across Z=0 it visited the nonnegative Z section first, then its lower Y section first. The old Forge jar fed the negative-Z Rift, taking it from `0.7` to `1.8`. The rebuilt jar fed the expected Rift from `0.9` to `2.0`. In the same section, a Rift inserted first received the overload while closing, even with a closer candidate. At exactly `1.0` the jar stayed intact. Overload removed the jar without dropping one or spawning an extra Rift when a recipient was present.
- The packaged Forge fixture verified that partial overlap with the intake box counts, exact face contact and dead Rifts are excluded, and four eligible Rifts share the intake rate. The depleted closing Rift contributed only what it had left. The critical Rift contributed `0.2` per tick while retaining its size. The existing Rift Jar gameplay regression also passed.

Evidence is under `run/production/runtime-qa-cycle155` in `build.log`, `tests.log`, `donor.log`, `runtime-old.log`, `runtime-new.log` and `runtime-regression.log`. ### Cycle 156 · Rift Jar discharge and empty-fill sync

- Discharge uses double-precision distance from the eligible player's feet to the jar center, as in the donor. Eligibility follows the asymmetric search box. The dose remains signed on either side of the five-block distance, with the donor's Zero and Power multipliers. Discharge takes at most the fill remaining. [Pinned Rift Jar discharge](https://github.com/Sollace/Psychedelicraft/blob/319a4ea0160d6600cffcd5f5cc9e363ceaab44a1/src/main/java/ivorius/psychedelicraft/block/entity/RiftJarBlockEntity.java#L80)
- In packaged Forge, positions `0.00000001` blocks either side of the radial zero gave opposite signed doses. The old float distance rounded both to zero. Box tests covered the truncated north side, partial hitbox overlap, the excluded top face, and signed doses inside the box but beyond radius five. Creative and spectator players received no dose. A jar starting at `0.6` retained `0.5994752` after one update with the lid at 52 percent. A `0.0002` remainder reached exactly zero.
- The jar sends a tile update when a positive fill empties even if Forge previously exposed that fill only through chunk data. Both clients received `0.0002` in a full chunk packet. After the server emptied the jar, the previous client still showed that fill 30 ticks later and the rebuilt client showed zero. The first control run sent an ordinary block update for the starting fill, which allowed the old client to receive zero via a queued tile packet. The final comparison isolated the full chunk packet. The new JVM regression also verifies one notification on the emptying tick and no duplicate on the following tick.

### Cycle 160 · Molotov launch and initial client motion

- The Forge projectile uses the donor's triangular spread on all three axes. The legacy throw entry point supplies the aim and the owner's horizontal motion. The final payload reserves `-1` for an absent owner and uses the donor's velocity clamp and 1/8000-block-per-tick precision. Clients can still read a spawn with an empty payload, as sent by earlier port builds. The saved owner UUID and later velocity updates retain their existing paths.

The previous jar reached the same wall on the same ticks in this scene, with different launch velocities.

### Cycle 163 · Trace the Nether chunk-generation warnings

- Reproduced the portal search's 17 × 17 chunk request order in a fresh Nether with seed `162`, using direct chunk requests. The fixture spawned no projectile and built no portal. With the default Forge settings, the run with Psychedelicraft and the run without it both produced 11 nested population warnings at the same chunk coordinates. The 256 populated chunks in each sample contained 4,461,400 netherrack, 30,356 quartz ore, 117,812 lava blocks and zero copper.
- Every warning stack reached the vanilla `WorldGenHellLava` neighbor lookup from the `NETHER_LAVA2` population pass in `ChunkGeneratorHell`. Forge picks the warning's mod name from the active container or the calling stack. During this test it named the QA fixture. During the portal test it named Psychedelicraft, whose projectile called vanilla dimension travel. The Overworld copper generator and projectile code were absent from the captured chunk request paths in the direct reproduction.
- A third fresh run with Psychedelicraft enabled Forge's `fixVanillaCascading` option for the sample. It produced zero nested population warnings. The block counts changed to 4,462,958 netherrack, 29,723 quartz ore and 117,607 lava blocks. Forge shifts the vanilla lava feature under that option. The port retains Forge's default setting.
- All three runs passed on muted Forge 14.23.5.2864. The packaged jar remained the Cycle 162 build, SHA-256 `27be68e1fe3649a56cd6b3acd1704542e1c68da6d8f149cd45ca4e0836d689f5`. Bamboo Jungle has no legacy equivalent, so jimsonweed's named defaults and the jungle tag share the same five legacy members. The shared feature switch and exclusion precedence apply to both agave and peyote.

## Repository and branch survey

The checkout initially used `1.21.5`; its working tree was clean. `origin` is [AuramMods/Psychedelicraft](https://github.com/AuramMods/Psychedelicraft), and the upstream is [Sollace/Psychedelicraft](https://github.com/Sollace/Psychedelicraft). After fetching origin, all 16 origin branch heads matched upstream's advertised heads. There was no existing 1.12 branch in either branch listing. Searching the fetched commit subjects for `1.12` also returned no matches; this does not rule out work in other forks or unpublished history.

The available branches were:

```text
master
1.19.2  1.19.3  1.19.4
1.20  1.20.1  1.20.1-lts  1.20.2  1.20.2-lts  1.20.4  1.20.4-lts
1.21  1.21.4  1.21.5  1.21.11
25w14craftmine
```

**1.21.5 is the fork's default checkout, but is not its highest numbered Minecraft branch.** `1.21.11` exists and has a newer head. It was inspected for build configuration and scope, not reviewed to the same depth as the two requested candidates.

| Branch | Head | Head date | Declared platform | Main Java files / lines | Assessment |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | ---: | --- |
| `master` | `1f19dfa6` | 2022-12-31 | Forge 1.8, Java 7; legacy APIs remain | 262 / 25,702 | Historical reference; uncertain build coherence |
| `1.19.2` | `8d5b0c9e` | 2023-08-23 | Fabric 1.19.2, Java 17 | 284 / 24,648 | Slightly smaller, but still crosses the same major platform boundaries |
| `1.20.1` | `319a4ea0` | 2024-06-13 | Fabric 1.20.1, Java 17 | 293 / 25,981 | Recommended initial donor |
| `1.20.1-lts` | `720118ae` | 2026-08-17 | Fabric 1.20.1, Java 17 | 433 / 40,650 | Better recent feature coverage; substantially larger port |
| `1.21.5` | `84b6eb29` | 2026-10-07 | Fabric 1.21.5, Java 21 | 433 / 40,527 | More native components, codecs, and renderer changes |
| `1.21.11` | `1636eeec` | 2026-10-12 | Fabric 1.21.11, Java 21 | 448 / 42,099 | Further from the target; reference only |

Dates are Git committer dates, not release dates. Counts cover tracked `src/main/java/**/*.java`; lines include comments and whitespace. They indicate the amount of source to inspect, not a time estimate. Full pinned source snapshots: [plain 1.20.1][plain], [1.20.1 LTS][lts], [1.21.5][modern], [legacy master][legacy], [1.21.11][newest].

### Why plain 1.20.1 beats 1.21.5 for this target

- The plain branch has **140 fewer Java files** and about **36% fewer Java lines**. Its recipe data is already checked in as JSON.
- Player and container persistence is directly expressed in NBT. In 1.21.5, `item/component/PSComponents.java` registers native `ComponentType` values with codecs and packet codecs. That architecture has no native counterpart in 1.12.2.
- Plain `network/MsgDrugProperties.java` carries an entity ID and NBT through Fabwork. The 1.21.5 messages use packet codecs, adding another translation layer before replacing Fabwork with Forge networking.
- Plain rendering uses `PostEffectProcessor`, direct framebuffer operations, and entity-based models. In 1.21.5, `PostEffectRenderer` takes a render `Pool`, `DrugRenderer` consumes `PlayerEntityRenderState` and `RenderTickCounter`, and additional shader pipeline mixins are present. Both need renderer work, but plain 1.20.1 provides fewer intervening abstractions.
- Java 8 still requires substantial rewriting in the plain branch: a textual scan found 37 record declarations there, versus 73 in LTS and 88 in 1.21.5. `var`, pattern matching, switch expressions, and newer library calls also require review. Lowering the compiler target alone is insufficient.

The older `1.19.2` saves only nine Java files and 1,333 lines compared with plain 1.20.1, while still using Fabric, Java 17, modern registries, transfer APIs, and shaders. That small reduction does not justify giving up the later fixes by default.

### What choosing plain 1.20.1 postpones

LTS is much more than a dependency refresh. Its source includes newer fluid pipes, pumps and valves; revised machine processing; richer item/fluid components; additional effect behavior; and expanded configuration, data generation, and rendering compatibility. For example, `GlassTubeBlock`, `PumpBlock`, `ValveBlock`, `FluidTransferUtils`, and `item/component/PSComponents` are absent from plain 1.20.1. Some apparent new classes are refactors of existing features, so file additions alone are not a feature list.

Useful maintenance commits in LTS include:

| Commit | Change to consult when porting the affected system |
| --- | --- |
| `96b26a13` | Bunsen burner item duplication fix |
| `4cad9e64` | Drying table crash during village generation |
| `c0947d68` | Crash when adding items to the vat |
| `a14f8496` | Reacting recipes consuming fluids incorrectly |
| `df47779e` | Crafting table recipe fixes |
| `e6a9a1a6` | Infinite loop while cannabis grows |
| `b847bfeb` | Packet serialization errors |

These are reference fixes, not a claim that every bug exists in the simpler branch: some concern systems introduced or rewritten later. Read each relevant diff and reproduce its behavior on the port before adapting it. Changes beyond LTS include `678cfa56` (dyed bottle crafting), `2fad24c4` (harmonium dyeing/undyeing), and `c7cabe7c` (bottle/cauldron interaction). A wholesale merge from a newer Minecraft branch would also import the platform dependencies being removed.

### What the legacy Forge branch offers

`master/build.gradle` declares Forge `1.8-11.14.3.1491`, ForgeGradle `1.2-SNAPSHOT`, Gradle 2.6, Java 7, and IvToolkit `1.2.1:dev`. However, `PSRegistryHandler` still imports `cpw.mods.fml` and uses APIs such as `setTextureName` and `GameRegistry.registerItem`. There are 57 Java files importing `cpw.mods.fml` and 90 importing IvToolkit. The branch also installs an FML core plugin and ASM transformers under `ivorius.psychedelicraftcore`.

It is therefore an old/incomplete migration reference, not a verified Forge 1.8 baseline. Its `PSRegistryHandler`, proxy classes, fluid classes, world generation, networking setup, and `client/rendering/shaders` can inform a 1.12 implementation. Its old coremod transformers and dependency configuration need fresh review rather than reuse unchanged. No legacy build was attempted during this investigation.

## Architecture and concrete port work

Paths below are relative to `src/main/java/ivorius/psychedelicraft/` on plain 1.20.1 unless another branch is named. These are implementation recommendations derived from source inspection, not completed replacements.

| Area and source touchpoints | Existing behavior | Forge 1.12.2 direction | Relative effort |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Psychedelicraft`, `client/PsychedelicraftClient`, `PS*` registration classes | Fabric entrypoints, callbacks, registry bootstrap | `@Mod`, Forge lifecycle/events, `RegistryEvent.Register`, separate client proxy | Medium |
| `entity/drug/DrugProperties`, `Drug`, `DrugType`, `influence/*`, `type/*` | Effect progression, attributes, stomach, persistence and sync | Preserve behavior/math; translate Minecraft types and attach player state through a capability | High |
| `mixin/MixinPlayerEntity`, `network/*` | Tick/save/load hooks; copy, join, dimension-change sync; two S2C message types | Capability persistence and Forge events; `SimpleNetworkWrapper` messages with main-thread handling | High |
| `fluid/*`, `fluid/container/*`, `fluid/physical/*` | Custom fluid state, fermentation, containers, Fabric transfer transactions | Forge fluids/`FluidStack`, explicit NBT payloads, `IFluidHandler` and item capabilities | High |
| `block/*`, `block/entity/*` | Crops, storage, processing, multiblocks, placed drinks | `Block`, `IBlockState`, metadata layouts, `TileEntity`, ticking and update packets | High |
| `recipe/*`, `screen/*`, `client/screen/*` | Custom recipe types/serializers, inventory interaction and progress displays | 1.12 recipe factories/`IRecipe`; machine recipe managers; `Container`, `GuiContainer`, `IGuiHandler` | High |
| `client/render/*`, `client/render/shader/*`, `mixin/client/*` | Post effects, world distortion, camera/hand changes, hallucinations and custom models | 1.12 rendering events, `GlStateManager`, framebuffer/shader integration, baked models and TESRs | Very high |
| `world/gen/*`, `entity/PSTradeOffers`, villager mixins | Biome modifications, configured features, structure pools, professions and tasks | Forge world generators, biome selection, legacy village integration and villager AI/trades | High |
| `command/*`, `PSDamageTypes`, `advancement/*` | Brigadier commands, damage registry keys, custom advancement criteria | `CommandBase`/`ICommand`, concrete `DamageSource` instances, 1.12 criteria | Medium |
| `config/*`, `compat/*` | JSON settings, Kirin GUI, Mod Menu and EMI integration | Retain settings semantics with Java 8-compatible serialization; Forge config GUI; later 1.12 recipe-viewer integration | Medium |

Forge's [registry documentation][registries], [capability documentation][capabilities], [networking documentation][networking], and [tile entity documentation][tileentities] describe the target mechanisms. In particular, 1.12 capability data needs explicit client synchronization; attaching persistent state does not replace the existing packets.

### Player state and packets

`DrugProperties.of(PlayerEntity)` currently casts to the mixin-supplied `DrugPropertiesContainer`. `MixinPlayerEntity` supplies tick and NBT hooks and modifies sleep and block-breaking behavior. `Psychedelicraft.onInitialize()` registers copy, join, and world-change callbacks. `sendCapabilities()` sends state to both surrounding players and the owning player; the method's name does not mean it already uses Forge capabilities.

The port must preserve owner and observer updates, tracking-start behavior, death/respawn copy rules, dimension changes, and reconnect persistence. Keep gameplay authoritative on the server and client visual state separated. Replace the two Fabwork messages (`MsgDrugProperties`, `MsgHallucinate`) with explicit message encoders/decoders. Forge 1.12 handlers execute on a network thread by default, so schedule access to game state on the appropriate main thread. [Target networking behavior][networking].

### Fluids and machines

This is more than renaming `FluidVariant` to `FluidStack`. Modern code stores fluid identity plus additional state and uses transactions for inventory/container changes. Forge's fill/drain simulation needs an adapter that preserves quantities, NBT, container remainders, and all-or-nothing changes where required.

Plain `FluidVolumes` already defines a bucket as **1,000 units**, a bottle as 2,000, a barrel as 16,000, and a vat as 32,000. LTS and 1.21.5 instead derive units from Fabric `FluidConstants`, with different barrel/vat capacities. Do not copy newer amounts or recipe timings without choosing the intended gameplay balance and converting units explicitly.

Inspect `Resovoir`, `VariantMarshal`, the fluid container classes, `BarrelBlockEntity`, `MashTubBlockEntity`, `DistilleryBlockEntity`, and `DryingTableBlockEntity` together. Preserve custom fluid data through bottling, pouring, block placement/breaking, saves, hoppers, partial transfers, and external automation. Simulation must not mutate state; refused or partial transfers must not duplicate or destroy contents.

### Blocks and content compatibility

Minecraft 1.12.2 still persists a block's metadata in **four bits**. Each ported block needs an explicit state-to-metadata design. Derive connection/render-only properties from neighbors, or store additional persistent data in a tile entity; use separate block IDs where appropriate. Directly carrying over every modern property combination can silently lose state after a reload. [Forge blockstate documentation][blockstates].

There are also vanilla-content gaps. Plain `PSItems` includes juniper hanging signs, and blocks use newer wood/sound APIs. Hanging signs, chest boats, waterlogging, newer materials, and modern villager job sites cannot be presumed available. Record an explicit substitute, custom implementation, or deferred feature for each such reference instead of silently dropping recipes or registrations.

### Rendering is the biggest technical uncertainty

The mod's defining behavior depends on `DrugRenderer`, `ShaderLoader`, `GeometryShader`, `LoadedShader`, `PostEffectRenderer`, and numerous client mixins. Plain 1.20.1 contains 51 files under its shader directory and 24 shader files declaring GLSL 150. It changes world geometry as well as applying full-screen effects.

1.12's render lifecycle, shader interfaces, vertex submission, and LWJGL environment differ from the modern code. GLSL text and effect formulas are useful inputs, but shader loading, attributes/uniforms, framebuffer ownership, camera transforms, and hook timing need a target-specific implementation. The legacy branch's shader wrappers provide historical reference, not evidence of compatibility with the target.

Prove one full-screen effect and one geometry/camera effect early. Test resolution changes, fullscreen toggles, resource reloads, effect stacking, and cleanup when effects end. A compiling jar or successful title screen would not establish that this part works. Decide whether any remaining hook requires an access transformer or a 1.12-compatible mixin bootstrap after checking actual Forge events; neither the Fabric mixin setup nor the old ASM transformers can be assumed to transfer intact. Optional rendering-mod support comes after a working vanilla Forge renderer and needs separate compatibility testing.

## Dependencies and build system

| Dependency | Plain 1.20.1 | 1.21.5 | Port treatment |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Java | 17 | 21 | Target Java 8 syntax, bytecode and available libraries |
| Gradle / Loom | 8.6 / `1.6-SNAPSHOT` | 8.12 / `1.10-SNAPSHOT` | Replace with a Forge MDK-based build |
| Fabric Loader / API | `0.14.21` / `0.85.0+1.20.1` | `0.16.10` / `0.119.6+1.21.5` | Replace integrations with target Forge/Minecraft APIs |
| Fabwork | `1.2.0` | `1.3.2+1.21` | Replace its packet abstraction |
| Kirin | `1.15.1` | `1.20.5+1.21.5` | Replace GUI helpers; newer branches also use its settings helpers |
| Terraform wood API | `7.0.0-beta.1` | Removed | Reimplement juniper wood/boat/sign integration for 1.12 |
| Mod Menu | `7.0.0-beta.2` | `14.0.0-rc.2` | Replace config entrypoint with Forge integration |
| Recipe viewer | EMI `1.0.19+1.20.1` | TlaApi plus optional EMI/REI paths | Reimplement optional integration against a verified 1.12 viewer API |
| Sodium / Iris | No dedicated compat source in plain branch | Dedicated shader mixins; Sodium enabled, Iris disabled in properties | Do not carry these modern integrations into the baseline |

The listed artifacts are declared for their modern branches; no 1.12 builds of them were established by this audit. Port their small used interfaces or replace the integration rather than assume those jars will load. Also review `org.joml`, Mojang serialization/datafixer utilities, newer Guava methods, and Java library calls: they are not all supplied by the target runtime in the same form.

### Verified Forge starting configuration

The [official 1.12.2 downloads page][forge-downloads] listed **14.23.5.2864** as latest and **14.23.5.2859** as recommended at investigation time. The downloaded [2864 MDK][mdk] was inspected directly. It ships:

- Forge dependency: `net.minecraftforge:forge:1.12.2-14.23.5.2864`.
- ForgeGradle: `net.minecraftforge.gradle:ForgeGradle:3.+`.
- Gradle wrapper: **5.6.4**.
- Java source/target: **1.8**.
- MCP mappings: channel `snapshot`, version `20171003-1.12`.
- IDE tasks documented in its README: `genIntellijRuns` and `genEclipseRuns`.

Use that coherent starting configuration for the first implementation experiment. Run the actual build/client/server tasks and address their concrete failures. Once dependency resolution succeeds, record and pin the resolved ForgeGradle version for repeatability. Do not mix the modern Loom wrapper, an old ForgeGradle 2 tutorial, and this ForgeGradle 3 MDK. These facts establish what the archive contains, not that its build has run successfully on this machine.

Replace `fabric.mod.json` with appropriate 1.12 `mcmod.info`/Forge metadata; review the access widener and mixin hooks individually; provide `pack.mcmeta` for the target. Replace Fabric remapping/publishing tasks, Outlet, and inherited Fabric/Quilt release declarations. Keep publishing credentials and upstream project identifiers out of the new release configuration until the fork's publishing destination is selected.

The inherited CI runs Gradle `check`; modern branches use their modern JDKs. There are no checked-in test files in the reviewed `master`, `1.19.2`, `1.20.1`, LTS, or `1.21.5` snapshots. `1.21.11` adds a client gametest source and metadata, which is not a ready-made 1.12 test harness. Update CI to the target toolchain and actual artifact build during implementation. The `1.21.5` README still says JDK 17 even though its build and CI specify 21, so build files are the more useful source for that branch's declared requirements.

## Assets, recipes, world generation and licensing

Plain 1.20.1 has **996 tracked main resource files**: 758 under `assets`, 234 under `data`, and four top-level files. Its data includes 91 mod recipes, 24 advancements, 45 mod loot tables, six structure templates, custom placed-drink definitions, tags, and auxiliary vanilla-extension worldgen/loot data. LTS and 1.21.5 have fewer checked-in resources because they use Java data-generation providers; that is not evidence of less content.

| Resource type | Reuse and conversion work |
| --- | --- |
| PNG textures, icons, OGG audio | Reuse payloads where appropriate; check paths, atlases, sound declarations and visual alignment in game |
| Model and blockstate JSON | Retain geometry where compatible; translate modern parents, texture references, predicates and state variants; rebuild dynamic drink/fluid models |
| Language JSON | Convert to UTF-8 `assets/psychedelicraft/lang/*.lang`, with translation keys matching target code; preserve formatting and multiline escaping |
| `data/*/recipes` | Convert supported crafting recipes to `assets/<namespace>/recipes`; implement custom factories and machine recipe loading explicitly |
| Tags | Map shared material categories to OreDictionary where suitable; implement other membership lists explicitly |
| Loot and advancements | Move to target-supported resource paths and translate schemas, conditions, functions and criteria; block drops need 1.12 block logic rather than modern block loot tables |
| Worldgen JSON and village structures | Recreate feature placement and legacy village integration; templates may contain modern block names/properties and jigsaw assumptions |
| Shader JSON/GLSL | Preserve effect intent and reusable shader math, but rewrite integration for the target renderer |

Forge 1.12 already supports JSON crafting recipes; it does **not** use the modern datapack layout. Its [recipe documentation][recipes] specifies `assets/<modid>/recipes` and `_factories.json` for custom types. Its [localization documentation][localization] specifies `.lang` files and the escaping rules. Merely moving every `data` file into `assets` will not convert its schemas or implement missing runtime loaders.

The repository's [LICENSE](../LICENSE) is MIT, with a 2022 Lukas Tenbrink copyright notice; older source files also carry attribution. Preserve the license and source notices when porting. The README additionally requests a link and credit to Ivorius. Keep upstream contributor credit, and check licenses independently before copying code from dependencies. This task did not alter attribution or publishing metadata.

## Suggested implementation sequence

1. **Establish the Forge application.** Replace the build with the inspected MDK setup, introduce a minimal Forge entrypoint and sided initialization, and get the packaged jar to load in an actual 1.12.2 client and dedicated server. Adapt or stage incompatible source as explicit work in progress; a stub jar is only a starting milestone.
2. **Prove the distinctive behavior.** Port one consumable, a small effect model, persistent player capability and server-to-client sync. Implement one post effect and one world/camera effect. Confirm behavior with two players, reconnect, death, and a dimension change. This exposes the highest-risk integration before translating hundreds of classes.
3. **Port the core content loop.** Implement crops, harvesting/drying, a container and barrel/mash/distillery chain, recipes, inventories, screens, custom fluids and automation. Verify quantities and NBT through partial transfers, crafting remainders, hoppers, saves and block breaking. Then expand the rest of the existing 1.20.1 content.
4. **Complete acquisition and world behavior.** Port generation, villages/trades, entities, loot, commands, advancements, sounds, translations and settings. Resolve modern-only vanilla references through a documented compatibility list.
5. **Review later improvements and release behavior.** Adapt applicable LTS/newer fixes, then add explicitly selected newer features and optional recipe-viewer/rendering compatibility. Update documentation and CI to the actual supported target. Test the reobfuscated production jar, not only development runs.

For focused automated coverage, prioritize effect progression/decay, NBT round trips, metadata round trips, fluid conservation, simulated operations, and container remainders. In-game checks should cover shader lifecycle, GUI shift-click behavior, multiplayer synchronization, save/reload, generated structures and dedicated-server class loading. These are proposed checks for implemented behavior; none were run as part of this documentation-only investigation.

## Decisions and remaining uncertainty

- **Initial parity:** proposed target is plain 1.20.1 gameplay, followed by selected later fixes/features. Full LTS parity is a larger alternative and would change the base recommendation.
- **Renderer scope:** the desired endpoint includes the characteristic visual effects. Which hooks need custom injection, and which graphics configurations can be supported, remains unproven until the early rendering experiment.
- **Modern-only content:** decide substitutes or custom backports for hanging signs, chest boats and other missing vanilla features as their systems are implemented; track omissions explicitly.
- **Saves:** assume new 1.12.2 worlds. Neither modern-world downgrading nor migration of this mod's modern saves has been designed. Preserve stable registry names where feasible and version the new NBT format.
- **Optional mods and release destination:** exact 1.12 integration versions, graphics-mod compatibility, and fork publishing targets remain to be selected. They are not prerequisites for starting the core Forge port.
- **Effort:** this is a multi-stage rewrite of platform integration, not a version bump. Source inspection supports the donor choice but cannot provide a reliable completion estimate until the first build/rendering/networking experiment runs.

## Investigation record and reproducibility

Completed: fetched origin; listed upstream branches and compared heads; inspected build files, dependencies, entrypoints, player state, networking, fluids, recipes, registries, shaders, mixins, resources, CI and relevant history; examined the legacy Forge source; downloaded and read the current 1.12.2 MDK; cross-checked target APIs against versioned Forge documentation.

Not performed: dependency resolution/builds of the donor branches, a Forge project build, game launches, rendering checks, multiplayer tests, or performance measurements. There is no claim that any inspected branch currently builds. No `docs/MANUAL_CHANGE_PROTOCOL.md` or repository `AGENTS.md` was present in the initial checkout or the selected donor snapshot. No `.codegraph` directory was present in the initial checkout. Temporary source snapshots and the MDK archive were kept outside the repository.
